| vendor (any), from M6 | any | any | any | refused, `engine_retired`: the sentence says the vendor engine left macOS at the release that retired it and names `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=cua` |
| an unknown value | | | | `invalid_setting`, one sentence naming the two values |

Where two refusals apply to one cell, they are checked in this order: an unknown value first, then the host setting (`repl_host_refused`), then the approved engine (`engine_not_installed`), then Node (`repl_host_node`); so an explicit `cua` with no approved engine is `engine_not_installed` whatever the Node version, and the unit cases cover every cell of the table, not only the rows written out.

With `cua`, the launcher sets `CUA_SKY_SERVICE` to cua's service module and `CUA_ENGINE_APP` to the approved engine, and `doctor` reports the selection, the bundle, its signature and the engine's permission state. The own-host rows work end to end at the end of M1b; M3 adds comparisons B and C and the engine-aware tooling, not the switch.

M6 removes the vendor side on macOS: the runtime archive download; the manifest layout keys `node`, `nodeRepl`, `moduleDir`, `cuaRepl`, `skyServiceApp`, `skyVendorService`, `browserVendorService`, `ipcClient` and `vendorManifest`; the compatibility executable and `CUA_REPL_HOST=vendor` on macOS. The archive's module tree is also where cells resolve their package imports (`CUA_REPL_NODE_MODULE_DIRS`: playwright, sharp, pngjs, jpeg-js, pixelmatch) and where `cua` loads `classic-level` for the Chrome profile directory map, so M6 supplies cua's own: `runtime/modules/package.json` with a lockfile pinning those packages at the versions the vendor tree carries, installed by `cua install` with `npm ci --omit=dev` into `$CUA_HOME/modules/<lock hash>/`, and `classic-level` moved into cua's own dependencies. After M6 the vendor engine cannot run on macOS at all (its only host is gone), so M6's repetition of the acceptance omits the vendor-side items and cites M5's sitting for them. That is the composite's macOS end state; #171 does the same for Linux.

### Install, signing and permissions

**The approved engine.** macOS raises a screen-capture confirmation, which only the owner can answer and which stops the mini's CI runner, the first time each new on-disk copy of a capturing app captures (the sibling spec's evidence on the vendor helper, 2026-10-17; `scripts/native-baseline/prepare.mjs` exists because of it). So there is exactly one engine location per Mac, at a fixed place no `CUA_HOME` moves (inside the default home, outside every other; M1a.4, Surprises): `~/Library/Application Support/cua/engine/cua Engine.app`, with `engine.sock`, `engine.lock`, `usage.json`, `pending.json` and `approved.json` beside it (no version segment in the path: the bundle carries its version, and a versioned directory would let an older version's resident engine keep running with its grants beside the new one, outside `cua engine stop`'s reach). `buildEngine` writes `pending.json {build, identity, builtAt}` when it assembles a bundle; nothing can observe the owner's confirmation, so the confirmation is proven by its effect: `cua engine approve`, run with the owner at the console, refuses while `status.connections > 0` (connections other than its own), then sends the engine `approve`, which calls the two prompting requests (`AXIsProcessTrustedWithOptions` with the prompt option, `CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess`) and returns the grants' state as the engine reads them. **The prompts do not appear for this engine**: on the mini neither did, for an engine that is `LSUIElement` and launched with `open -n -g` (the first series' `approve` read both grants false at that request; `spike/approve/approve.json`), and the owner added the bundle by hand. The second sitting read why (U7, a fresh bundle id, `spike/sit2/u78-tcc.jsonl`): the engine's Accessibility request reached tccd and, within about 40 ms, `universalAccessAuthWarn` set the record to Denied (System Set) (20:27:50.98Z) with no window shown; the inference, marked as one, is that an `LSUIElement` app launched with `-g` has no window the warning process can present the prompt for, so it is denied at once. The fresh id's first capture after approve's relaunch did raise replayd's user-acknowledgement alert (`SCAlert`, 20:38:30.74Z): the owner reports clicking Allow, replayd logged "user acknowledgement refused" eleven seconds later, and both are kept, unreconciled; the engine's `captured: true` came before the alert resolved. So the command leads the by-hand path instead of waiting on a prompt (and M4 tries one lead: making the approve step's requests from a foreground launch, so the prompt has a window to attach to): for each grant that reads false it prints the exact steps (System Settings → Privacy & Security → Accessibility, → Screen & System Audio Recording and → Input Monitoring; the "+" button; the approved bundle's path; the third grant is the intervention tap's, and without it the engine runs with intervention detection off and says so, "User intervention") and polls `status` until the grant reads true or the operator confirms it at the console (a keypress; the read is not relied on, because a running engine read both grants on the mini before any relaunch, probe (f)'s f1, while on the MacBook Screen Recording read true only after one); then it sends `shutdown`, relaunches the engine with `open -n -g -a`, polls `status` until it answers, and sends `approve` again, which now takes one display capture that it discards, raising the capture confirmation the owner answers; on `captured: true` the command writes `approved.json {build, identity, approvedAt}`. No app is bound for it, so the step needs no scene. Harnesses, `doctor` and the selection table's "installed" column read `approved.json` only: a bundle whose `build` or `identity` differs from `approved.json`'s is pending, not installed. Every session and every harness home on that Mac serves the approved engine in place; a harness never installs its own copy, and `--approved-engine` on the harnesses is the analogue of `--approved-runtime`. Replacing it (a new `build`, or a signature that no longer matches the configured identity, which is the second reinstall trigger) is done only by `buildEngine`, and the harnesses refuse capture cells against a pending build. What re-raises the confirmation was measured twice. In the first sitting both grants survived a staged rebuild at the same path and a copy at a new path (same identity, whose designated requirement names the certificate's subject), on the mini and on the MacBook, but the mini re-raised the confirmation for each of two staged rebuilds at the approved path and not for the new-path copy, while the MacBook raised it for neither. The second sitting (U8, `spike/sit2/u8/probe-f.json`, `approve/owner.txt`, the sitting notes) ran the three moves in one hold with replayd's log and TCC's streaming and the approvals plist read before and after each: the same-path replacement of the sitting's build (b99ea967 over e3547c25), the staged same-path rebuild (a25da09a) and the new-path copy each came up with both grants kept and **raised no confirmation** (replayd logged no `SCAlert` line, tccd no "Notifying for access", the console no window), so the first sitting's asymmetry did not reproduce and stays unexplained but unrepeated. The signal from now on is replayd's `SCAlert` user-acknowledgement lines in the unified log (`+[SCAlert userAcknowledgementAlertWithBundleID:]`), which the fresh-id capture of U7 did produce; replayd's `ScreenCaptureApprovals.plist` carried no engine entry at any of its four reads and is not a signal. The install rule follows the evidence: **`buildEngine` expects no confirmation for a rebuild under the same identity**, streams replayd's log around the first capture of the new build and, if an `SCAlert` line appears, stops and tells the operator that the console must answer it, so a recurrence is detected rather than assumed; `cua install --engine` therefore needs no one at the Mac in the ordinary case.

`cua install` builds the engine when the engine is selected (`CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=cua`, or the default once M5 flips it) or when asked with `--engine`; otherwise it leaves the engine alone and `doctor` shows an `engine` row saying so, which keeps today's gates (`scripts/sandbox-probe/hosted-gate.sh`, `scripts/native-baseline/prepare.mjs`) running unchanged until they opt in. A build failure (no identity, no Xcode) fails `cua install` only when `--engine` was explicit; under the default it leaves the rest of the install done and prints the engine row's sentence. Building means `swift build -c release --scratch-path ~/Library/Application Support/cua/engine/build` in `native/engine/`, assembling and signing `cua Engine.app` in a staging directory beside the approved location (`<engine directory>/staging/`) with the identity named by `CUA_SIGNING_IDENTITY` or `~/.config/cua/engine.json`'s `signingIdentity` (a common name or SHA-1 from `security find-identity -v -p codesigning`), hardened runtime, no entitlements, and then, if a bundle with another `build` is at the location, sending its engine `shutdown` and only on success moving the old bundle aside and the staged one into place (two renames; the engine is stopped by then, so nothing serves in the gap) and writing `pending.json`. A `busy` answer fails the build visibly, naming the connections, with the approved bundle, `approved.json` and the running engine untouched and the staged bundle discarded. The order matters: assembling in place first would leave the resident engine, which exits only on `shutdown` or logout, serving the socket beside a bundle every new client rejects on its `build` check and that `cua engine approve` could never approve (the ordinary outcome of an update on the mini, where a session usually holds a connection), and rewriting a running executable's signed pages kills it mid-session. A bundle whose `build` already matches is left as it is (reinstall changes nothing). `cua engine build [--sign adhoc]` is the command form of `buildEngine` (M1b); `cua install --engine` (M4) calls the same function. The identity is the owner's Apple Development certificate (ruling 2026-10-18): its designated requirement names the certificate's subject, so a rebuild signed by the same certificate keeps the Accessibility grant, and the subject is the same string on every Mac the owner's Apple ID issues a certificate to, so each Mac may hold its own certificate. Without a configured identity a build refuses with the sentence that names the setting; ad-hoc signing (`cua engine build --sign adhoc`, `cua install --engine-sign adhoc`) exists for the hosted CI runner, where no permission is granted anyway, and is never a default: ad-hoc signatures change with every build and would lose the grants on each rebuild. The repository carries no certificate name. Signing over SSH (every harness build on the mini runs over SSH) cannot happen in the SSH session: loginwindow unlocks the login keychain only for the GUI login, an SSH session sees it locked (`codesign` fails `errSecInternalComponent`), and an unlock made in one SSH connection does not carry to the next, so that route would need the owner's password at every rebuild (Decision Log, 2026-10-20). So when `buildEngine` runs without the GUI session's keychain (`launchctl managername` is not `Aqua`, as in every SSH session), it runs the `codesign` step as a one-shot launchd job bootstrapped into the user's GUI domain (`launchctl bootstrap gui/<uid>`, a bounded wait for its exit, then `bootout`), where the keychain is unlocked; the job reads the identity from the setting itself, so no command line carries it, and with no GUI login the build fails with the sentence naming it. Two owner steps make that sign silent, once per Mac and key: the key's partition list admits `codesign` (`security set-key-partition-list`, done on the mini 2026-10-19), and the private key's access list trusts `codesign`, which the owner grants at the console by answering codesign's first key prompt with the login password and **Always Allow**. Until then the GUI-domain sign raises that prompt on the console and waits; `buildEngine` bounds the wait, boots the job out and fails with a sentence saying a keychain prompt from `codesign` is waiting on that Mac's console for the owner, because the prompt outlives the job (Surprises). The first GUI-domain sign on a Mac therefore runs only with the owner at the console, inside a hold on the mini. Both steps are recorded in `docs/onboarding-new-mac.md` by M4 with the exact commands; automation never enters the password.

Permissions are requested where they are first needed and reported, never clicked: the first observation calls `AXIsProcessTrustedWithOptions` with the prompt option and fails `permissionsPending` (−10014) with a sentence naming "cua Engine" in System Settings → Privacy & Security → Accessibility; the first screenshot calls `CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess` the same way. Because the prompt may not appear (the mini, above), both sentences name the by-hand path, the pane, the "+" button and the bundle's path, not only the prompt. Because macOS applies a Screen Recording grant only to a relaunched process, and whether a running process observes its own grant differed between the two Macs in M0 (the mini's engine read it before a relaunch, the MacBook's only after), neither flow depends on observing it: after a screenshot has failed `permissionsPending`, the service, on the next screenshot request, sends `shutdown` to the engine (a `busy` answer, another connection open, leaves it running and the request fails again with the sentence), relaunches it through the relay and retries the screenshot once; until a relaunch has happened screenshots keep failing with the `permissionsPending` sentence, which says a relaunch is pending. On the mini the owner grants all three at the GUI console over Screen Sharing, by hand in System Settings, once per signing identity, plus the capture confirmation when replayd raises one. `cua doctor` reports the three grants from the engine's `status` request (Input Monitoring as the intervention tap's, with detection off when it is missing), the signing identity, whether the socket's owner is the approved bundle, and the probe mode's ability.

### Verification system

The owner's core requirement: no regression across the replacement, proven, before any default changes. The system has seven parts, built in the milestones that first need them and run in full at M5. Every live run begins and ends with `cua engine stop` (the `shutdown` request to the approved engine, never a signal; a `busy` answer, or `status.connections > 0` at preparation, refuses the run naming the connections, which is the hold discipline's job to prevent) under `prepare.mjs`'s admission lock, and with the vendor helper stopped as `prepare.mjs` does today, so a cold row is "first launch by this run" on both sides. Harnesses never call `buildEngine`: the sitting's operator builds and approves with the owner present, and every run computes `build` from its own checkout and refuses unless it equals both the attested and the approved `build`, so a sitting can never attest an engine built from older source.

1. **Three comparisons on the native baseline harness.** The vendor engine accepts only the vendor host, so "same host, different engine" is possible only on the vendor host, where cua's service runs inside the vendor trusted worker (loaded through `CUA_SKY_SERVICE`; the vendor relay connects to any absolute socket path). `scripts/native-baseline.mjs run` takes `--host` and `--engine` independently (`--host vendor --engine cua` is the loaded-service lane) and records the engine attestation from `_meta['cua/nativeEngine']`, with the helper attestation classifying both `SkyComputerUseService` and `cua-engine` processes. Three `compare` modes, each judging every row the default compare judges (per operation, the candidate's median within baseline + max(10%, 500 ms), p95 and max reported, three attested runs per side with every planned sample present, else NO VERDICT) and each naming exactly which attested fields may differ:
   - **A, engine and service**: `--host vendor --engine vendor` against `--host vendor --engine cua`; host and sandbox equal, engine and service differ (both services make a policy round trip and an elicitation per call; `cua/serviceMs` on both lanes separates the host's transport from the service-and-engine time, and `cua/engineMs` on cua's lane prices its service alone when attribution matters).
   - **B, host only**: `--host vendor --engine cua` against `--host cua --engine cua`; engine and service equal, host and sandbox differ. Prices the own host's relay (every socket chunk crosses the supervisor as base64 JSONL) and sandbox.
   - **C, end to end**: `--host vendor --engine vendor` against `--host cua --engine cua`; the product verdict, labelled as two variables.
   A fresh vendor triple is taken in the same sitting as each candidate triple; the 2026-10-14 runs remain a reference, not a side. Every macOS computer run passes `--approved-runtime` and `--approved-engine`, as the harness requires. The baseline's key cells pass through the #162 key guard, which sends keys only while TextEdit is frontmost; the vendor satisfies it because its launch and click paths activate TextEdit, cua's engine never would. So every lane gains a harness-owned, not-judged `setup.activate` step (device-side `open -a TextEdit`, verified with `lsappinfo front`) right after `bind.getApp` and before `setup.newDocument`, whose `super+n` path is the first guarded key, in `scripts/native-baseline/macos.mjs`: **the baseline comparisons measure the driven-app-frontmost regime**, which is also the vendor's; non-interference is measured only by part 5's paired cells. The no-regression gate judges wall time as `compare` does; the 10% "beat" of part 5 and acceptance 5 is read from the in-cell columns, since about 20 ms of transport per call would dilute a margin on a 51 ms row.
2. **Scenes.** Four harness-owned windows, one per app family, each with run-owned content, each in a process addressed by a path that names exactly one process on either engine (the facade binds a string, and the vendor engine resolves it too, so a harness instance of an app the owner also runs could never be named): TextEdit (AppKit text); Finder (AppKit lists, a run-owned folder with 200 files; Finder's process is the system's, only its window is the run's); **Chrome for Testing**, downloaded by the harness from Google's Chrome for Testing endpoint at the version and SHA-256 pinned in `scripts/native-matrix/fixtures/chrome-for-testing.json` (`scripts/spike/engine/fixtures/` in M0) and unzipped under `~/Library/Caches/cua-harness/chrome-for-testing/<version>/` on the device (the hosted lane's `hosted.sh` uses the image's preinstalled copy; nothing pins or places one on a Mac today), with its own `--user-data-dir` under the run directory (its bundle id `com.google.chrome.for.testing` is not the owner's Chrome's; it shows Chromium's own UI and a loopback page the harness serves; the owner's Chrome is never observed, driven or killed); and **an Electron fixture app** the harness builds from the `electron` dev dependency pinned in `package.json` from M0, a copy of the dependency's `Electron.app` whose `Info.plist` is rewritten with its own `CFBundleIdentifier` (`io.github.ssfskim.cua.fixture.electron`) and name and which is then re-signed ad hoc, since the plist edit invalidates the shipped signature (`scripts/spike/engine/fixtures/electron/` in M0, promoted under `scripts/native-matrix/fixtures/` in M1b: a text field, a list, a button; a scratch user-data directory), so the Electron and Chromium accessibility path is proven on a process the harness owns rather than on an owner app with owner content, and the stock `Electron.app` id that MAWS's dev builds on the mini share is never used. The refusal discipline: the harness refuses to start while a process with Chrome for Testing's or the fixture's bundle id is running that it did not start, while TextEdit is running (as the baseline's `prepare.mjs` refuses today), and while Finder, which always runs and is never the harness's process, has any window that is not the run's; the #162 key guard compares the frontmost process by pid, not bundle id, from M1b. Each scene's window is the driven process's only window. Scenes pair for the non-interference cells: the user window of a cell is the scene window of another family (TextEdit ↔ Finder, Chrome ↔ TextEdit, Electron ↔ Finder), so the driven app and the user's app are never the same process; the concurrent-use cell uses TextEdit as the user window with Finder and Chrome for Testing as the driven apps, and its sheet variant, which needs a driven app that opens a sheet on `super+w`, drives TextEdit with the Electron fixture's text field as the user window; the user window's value is read back through the probe mode's `focus` command (its `valueHash`), never through either engine. Breadth is the ruling, scenes are the proof: the engine must handle any ordinary app; the harness proves it on these. The scenes' fixed-state definitions, refusal checks and the two fixture launchers are written in M0 for the spike and promoted into the harness in M1b; M0 also confirms that the vendor engine binds a path-addressed app to exactly that process.
3. **Differential suite and goldens.** `scripts/repl-parity.mjs run --suite native-mac --host … --engine …` (M2b adds the `native-mac` suite and `--engine` to `parseSelection`), the same scripted cells on both engines, then `diff <vendor> <cua> --expected <declared>` (its JSON gains a `summary` line, "N unexpected differences"): the accessibility text on the scenes' own windows is compared **modulo index renumbering** (indexes replaced by order of appearance everywhere they occur, in element lines, in the focused-element trailer and in the diff's `Removed element IDs:` ranges; the vendor's lines, normalized to role, flags, title and labels, must appear in cua's text in the same order; lines cua adds are allowed only when the declared file names each added element by role and title and no golden declares more than 10% added lines without a Decision Log entry) and structurally where a window could carry the user's content. The same rule pins the renderer's goldens under `test/native-goldens/`: both engines' outputs recorded on the same scene states in one sitting, so drifting state cannot separate them. Expected differences are declared, as `vendor-defects.mjs` declares them today, never silently absorbed.
4. **Method matrix.** The 11 methods × the four scenes plus TextEdit's Open panel (an out-of-process target), in two regimes: **frontmost** (the driven app activated by a harness `setup.activate`, the vendor's regime, where the vendor's outcome is the reference) and **background** (the paired user window of part 5 frontmost, where the reference is the fixture's ground truth, since the vendor's activated-app outcome is not the reference there). Every cell is judged by outcome, equal sample for sample or strictly better (the Linux `method.*` rule): the value, caret, selection or list state read back through `getAXState` against what the action should have produced, the error sentences for refused cases (select on a non-text element, set on a non-settable one, an invalid index, an app with no window) and the `cua/inputRoute` that carried each action. The background regime includes the launch path for the scenes the engine can launch into their fixed state (TextEdit, whose run-owned document the harness opens; the Electron fixture only if it derives its scratch user-data path itself; never Chrome for Testing, whose `--user-data-dir` and loopback page only the harness can give it): `resolve_app` of the app while it is not running must bring it up without the engine activating it, and a family whose app activates itself at launch, which M0 probe (a) measures, is recorded for the owner's acceptance like a route-4 family rather than counted as a failure the engine cannot change. The regime's cells are the non-interference cells of part 5, so an engine whose background posts land nowhere (the Chromium case route 3 exists for) fails the outcome rule rather than scoring non-interference 100%. `scripts/native-matrix.mjs run --device --host --engine` runs it for one engine and `compare`s two runs; it also writes the per-scene speed cells and the three-point summary below.
5. **The three points, defined.** *Speed*: on every scene, in-cell medians of `getAXState`, `getScreenshot`, `listApps` and each method cell, from the matrix runs, lower than the vendor's by at least 10% with p95 not worse, three runs per side; the no-regression gate of part 1 stays the inherited rule, this is the stricter "beat", and a missed beat on a scene is an owner fork at M5 (accept parity there, or continue). *Finding elements*: per scene a fixed list of ten targets drawn from the fixtures' ground truth before either engine's text is seen (every control the harness put into the Electron fixture and the loopback page; a fixed set of Finder and TextEdit controls named from the fixture definitions), each to be resolved by name through the model's path (`getAXState` text search → index → action), plus the share of element lines with a non-empty title or label and the `ID:` count; the vendor's misses are then a result, not the sampling frame. The vendor's scores are recorded first, cua's are not lower on any measure, and the sum of resolved targets is higher, or every measure is equal, in which case the report records parity and the owner decides. Where cua resolves what the vendor misses, its text carries lines the vendor's lacks; the Chrome and Electron scenes were therefore expected to exceed part 3's 10% added-line cap, and the Decision Log entry that cap requires is written when the first such golden is recorded, not discovered at M5. *(2026-10-19, M1a.3: on the mini the vendor exposed both Chromium pages fully and M1a's goldens add 0 lines on every scene, so no such entry was needed for them; the cap and its entry rule stand for later recordings; Surprises.)* *Not interfering*: for each paired scene cell (a background-regime cell of part 4) the harness activates the user window, drives the scene's app, and requires the cell's outcome under part 4's rule and three things more: after every action, read from the device side and independently of either engine, the frontmost application (`lsappinfo front`, the existing `FRONTMOST_SCRIPT` in `scripts/accept/key-guard.mjs`) and the topmost on-screen window's owner pid and bounds (`CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` through a Swift script, which needs no permission when names are not read) are unchanged; during the whole cell, a resident device-side watcher (the probe mode's `watch-activations`, subscribed to `NSWorkspace.didActivateApplicationNotification`) recorded no activation of any app (the probe instances are launched with `-g` and their pids, which they print, are excluded by the watcher and the frontmost oracle), so a steal handed back inside an action (route 3's active-state events, the vendor's `SystemFocusStealPreventer`) is seen; and `cua/inputRoute ≠ 4` outside an owner-accepted family. The vendor's score is recorded on the same cells. The #162 key guard, which refuses keys unless the target is frontmost, gains one allowed case for these cells: the frontmost window is the harness's own user window of this run, identified by owner pid and bounds; it stays as strict as today for every other window. A further cell, **concurrent use**, has a physical-equivalent keystroke delivered to the user window during a driven action and requires the driven action's text to land in the driven app (read back through `getAXState` against the text sent) and the keystroke to land in the user window (read back from the user window's value). The driven action is a long `typeText` so the interval is wide; the result's `cua/actionInterval` carries the action's start and end times, the probe prints its post time, and a sample whose post does not fall inside the interval is invalid, not a pass. A variant of the cell has the driven action open a sheet (`super+w` on a dirty TextEdit document, with the Electron fixture's text field as the user window, since neither Finder nor Chrome for Testing opens a sheet on `super+w`) while the keystroke lands in the user window, the case the intervention rule must not fire on. In both, the user window's value is read back by `cua-engine --probe focus`, which reports the SHA-256 of the focused element's value when the focused window is the harness's own. In the spike the keystroke is the owner's over Screen Sharing. In the suite it comes from the approved engine bundle's `--probe` mode (`cua-engine --probe keystroke`, a separate instance launched through `open -n -g`, posting from an HID-state event source at the HID event tap, and refusing to post unless `FrontWindowProbe` shows the harness's user window frontmost): a plain script over SSH could not post, since TCC attributes it to sshd and drops the event silently, and the approved bundle's grants are the only granted identity the harness has; `doctor` reports the probe's ability, and the matrix refuses the cell rather than passing it vacuously when the probe cannot post. Two cells test the rule's positive side after M0, since the concurrent-use cells only prove it must not fire: **intervention fires**, in which the harness activates the driven scene's window (its own), the probe, gated on that window, posts one keystroke into it during a driven action, and the result must be `userIntervened` with the next action on that app refused until a `get_app_state` (the `userIntervened` error carries `cua/actionInterval` as a result would, and a sample whose post falls outside the interval is invalid, as in the concurrent-use cell); and **pre-action refusal**, in which the probe posts a keystroke into the frontmost driven window and the harness requests `typeText` at once, which must be refused as `userIntervened` with the still-interacting sentence and nothing posted (the window's value equal to its value after the probe's keystroke). Because the harness learns the post time only after the probe exits and its request then crosses SSH, MCP, the supervisor, the worker and the socket, the cell has a validity rule like the concurrent-use cell's: every input result and every refusal carries `cua/receivedAt` (the engine's receipt time) and `cua/hidIdleMs` (the milliseconds since the last user event it attributed to the driven app at receipt, "User intervention"), and a sample whose receipt is more than the debounce after the probe's printed post time is invalid, not a verdict. To make valid samples reachable at all (a fresh `ssh` per harness command, then the MCP call, can approach the debounce on their own), the harness launches the probe without `-W` with `keystroke --at <unix ms>`, a post scheduled a little ahead, and sends the `typeText` so that it arrives just after that time. A cell whose samples are all invalid is NO VERDICT, which blocks M5 like a missing row, and the owner's fork there is to carry it as an owner-run item. The vendor's result is recorded on both. The probe posts from an HID-state event source (`CGEventSourceCreate(.hidSystemState)`), never from the engine's private source, so its post resets the HID idle counter as a user's keystroke does. Whether an HID-tap-posted event resets the counter and is seen by the tap was answered in the second sitting (yes to both, 3 of 3; "User intervention"), so the positive cells stay in the matrix; had it not been, both positive cells would have become owner-run acceptance items (a keystroke over Screen Sharing), recorded as such rather than silently absent.
6. **Korean and Unicode matrix.** `typeText` and `paste` of: `The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog.`, `한글 테스트`, `ㄱㄴㄷ`, `가나다 abc 123`, `😀 이모지`, `é` as e plus U+0301, `日本語`, and `줄1⏎줄2` (⏎ a Return key event), into the TextEdit scene, the Chrome loopback page's textarea and the Electron scene's text field, run twice: with the Latin input source active and with the Korean source active (the mini's system language is Korean, so both sources exist; the probe mode's `input-source` command selects one through `TISSelectInputSource`; the matrix writes the owner's source and the per-context input preference into the run directory before the first switch and its `cleanup` restores both unconditionally, so a run that dies mid-way still leaves the owner's settings as it found them), once more with a Hangul composition in progress in the user window, and once more with the per-context input preference on and the driven app holding a Korean context while the session's source is Latin (the driven app's source set while it was frontmost, then the user window activated), the case the Text rule routes to paste, and once more with the probe's `input-source` command switching the session's source to Korean during a long keyed `typeText`, which must stop with `inputSourceChanged` and an entered-character count equal to the length of the window's value. One `pressKey` row also runs under each source: `r` then `k` into the same three fields must enter `rk` under the Korean source as under the Latin one, because the vendor's pid-posted keys bypass the input method (Surprises, MAWS P4-B2) and a key name is a key, not a composition. Every row is read back by SHA-256 inside the cell and compared with the expected string. A mismatch is a failed row; a failed row with a named cause (autocorrection, an app that ignores Unicode events) is declared in the expected file with that cause, never silently absorbed.
7. **Unit goldens and tests.** The renderer and diff against recorded `Skyshot` trees and the vendor's recorded text (part 3's rule); a settle cell, "the observation right after an action equals the observation two seconds later", scored on both engines in the differential suite; protocol framing and the 8 MiB bound; the policy module (forbidden list, risk) and the supervisor's approval memory (the elicitation shape against the harness matcher, a repeat answered from memory, a new connection asking again); the selection table above (`test/runtime-launch.test.mjs`, `scripts/repl-parity/selection.mjs` cases, every cell, including the three `engine_not_installed` rows and the refusal precedence); secret substitution shapes unchanged and the `concealed` mark (`test/services-sky*.test.mjs`); the untrusted-engine error path (`permissionsPending` reaching the model with its sentence: through a fake engine on hosted CI, whose runner grants the engine Accessibility, and live on the mini, M4's onboarding; corrected 2026-10-20, Decision Log, M1a.5).

Gates: the hosted macOS runner (`.github/ci-kit.yml`) builds the engine and runs its Swift unit tests (`engine-swift`) and 7 on every PR, the untrusted path through a fake engine (the runner grants Accessibility); the live parts (1–6) run on the mini inside a CI-opt hold, GUI, with the owner's one-time grants and the approved build. The default flips to `cua` (M5) only when 1–6 pass with three attested runs on each side. After M5 the vendor side is no longer the reference: M6 checks in M5's attested cua triple, its matrix runs and its parity run as `docs/evidence/<sitting date>-own-native-engine/baseline/` and gives parts 1, 3 and 5 same-engine forms: `compare --same-engine <baseline> <candidate>` (every default-plan row under the same limit; the permitted moves are `compareSameHost`'s list plus the engine `build`, `engineVersion` and the commit, which every engine change moves by definition), `repl-parity.mjs diff <baseline> <candidate>` for part 3, and the matrix's `compare` reading the speed and finding-elements measures as not worse than the baseline's. From then on every plugin release that carries a change to `native/engine/` or `src/services/engine/` reruns the live parts on the mini against that baseline before it ships (per release, not per PR: each rerun needs a newly approved build, which is a capture confirmation with the owner at the console), and the baseline is re-recorded, with a dated Decision Log entry, only when a macOS update or an accepted engine change moves it.

## Acceptance

Behaviour a person can verify on the mini, at the plugin release M5 ships (1–9) and M6 ships (10, with 1–4, 8 and 9 repeated; 5–7 cite M5's sitting, since the vendor engine no longer runs after M6):

1. `cua doctor --json` shows `engine.selected: "cua"`, `engine.bundle` at the approved location, `engine.signature.identity` matching the configured identity, `engine.build` equal to `approved.json`'s, and `engine.permissions.accessibility`, `screenRecording` and `inputMonitoring` all `true` (the third added by the owner's decision of 2026-10-20, Decision Log, "second input sitting").
2. In a session with the computer surface, `cua.getApp("com.apple.TextEdit")` asks one approval, answered by the harness in `verify.mjs --engine cua --bind com.apple.TextEdit` (and by the plugin's Elicitation hook in the owner's Claude Code sitting, M5), a second bind in the same connection asks nothing, and the processes holding cua's sockets and running under cua's runtime root are `cua-engine` and cua's Node: no `SkyComputerUseService` or `node_repl` started by cua (other installations' helpers, such as the ChatGPT app's own, are excluded by path).
3. With a Finder window frontmost and a TextEdit document behind it, `textedit.click({element_index: <the text view>})` places the caret; the Finder window is still frontmost and still has keyboard focus (`cua-engine --probe focus` reports Finder as the focused application and the run's window as its focused window, and a probe keystroke moves Finder's selection to a run-owned file while the document's value is unchanged), and the result's `cua/inputRoute` is not 4.
4. `app.typeText("한글 테스트 😀")` into a TextEdit document lands exactly, read back from the tree's `Value:`, with the Latin source active and with the Korean source active.
5. `node scripts/native-baseline.mjs compare --engines <vv> <vc>` (A) and `compare --end-to-end <vv> <cc>` (C) each print `VERDICT: PASS` with no judged row over the limit, and A's table shows cua's in-cell medians for `warm.getAXState`, `warm.getScreenshot` and `task.total` within the no-regression rule, with the 10% beat on each as the three-point report records it or, where a beat was missed, the owner's parity decision recorded in the Decision Log.
6. `node scripts/repl-parity.mjs diff <vendor-run> <cua-run> --expected scripts/repl-parity/expected/native-mac.json` for the `native-mac` suite prints `summary: 0 unexpected differences`.
7. `docs/evidence/<sitting date>-own-native-engine/summary.md`, written by `scripts/native-matrix.mjs summary`, shows every method equal or better on the four scenes and the Open panel, the finding-elements measures not lower with the resolved-target sum higher (or parity, recorded), non-interference 100% for cua with no route-4 action outside owner-accepted families, the concurrent-use cells passing, and the two positive intervention cells showing `userIntervened` and the pre-action refusal (or, where M0 probe (d) found that HID-tap posts do not reset the counter, the owner-run items recorded in the sitting).
8. `scripts/accept-native.mjs --engine cua --live-textedit --report <file>` on a scratch `CUA_HOME` installed with `--archive` (serving the approved engine): items 1–8, 10 and 11 PASS; items 12 and 13, which check the vendor host and its compatibility route, run pinned to `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=vendor` until M6 and are retired by M6; item 9's fresh-onboarding check, split out as its own item in M4, PASS on the mini after the owner revokes the grants in System Settings (or runs `tccutil reset Accessibility io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine` and the same for `ScreenCapture`; an owner action, never automation) and grants them again at the prompts.
9. `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=vendor` still runs a session on the vendor engine and host, attested as such (after M6: refused with the retirement sentence).
10. After M6, `cua install` on a fresh `CUA_HOME` on macOS downloads no runtime archive and installs cua's own module tree, `cua doctor` lists no vendor rows, a cell can still `import('playwright')` and `cua profiles` still maps Chrome profile directories, and `git grep -lE 'skyServiceApp|skyVendorService|ipcClient|SKY_CUA_SERVICE_PATH|2DC432GLL2' src` lists only the Linux pin's manifest and the launch lines that serve it.

## Plan of Work

Constraints that bind every milestone: no third-party code or package dependency in `native/engine/` (Apple frameworks only; Apple SPI by `dlsym` with feature detection); never launch, signal or kill a vendor helper, the owner's Chrome, the ChatGPT app or the owner's apps outside the harnesses' own fixtures and instances; GUI runs on the mini only inside a CI-opt hold, headless runs only with the runner idle; the mini's resident cua is updated only by the dispatching session with the owner present; permission dialogs and capture confirmations are answered by the owner at the GUI console, never by automation and never over SSH, TCC is never edited by automation, and automation stops at any password prompt; every run that changes the input source restores the owner's; nothing logs accessibility text, titles or screenshots of windows the harness does not own; the model-facing API and `docs/cua-api/core.md` stay unchanged except where M4 revises a rule the engine makes false; a change that moves a sandbox fingerprint re-records `docs/evidence/2026-10-18-hosted-macos-gate/baseline/` in the same PR; gates run through ci-kit on the hosted runner; each milestone lands as its own PR after a reviewer-high review and `MERGEABLE`; the spec's Progress, Surprises and Decision Log are current at every stopping point; a plugin bump (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`) accompanies every plugin-visible change (M5, M6).

### M0 — Reading program and measured spike

A prototyping milestone whose deliverable is knowledge. Two halves run in parallel; the reading half landed first (`F-helper-logic.md`, 2026-10-18) and the design sections above were revised from it (Decision Log entry 9) while the spike was being built, so the spike builds against the vendor's recipes rather than the earlier inferences.

**Reading.** Tier 1 (done 2026-10-18): `E-helper-catalogue.md`. Tier 2: Ghidra headless analysis of the pinned executable, already imported and analysed as project `sky` under `~/tools/ghidra-projects/` with the macOS natives built (`cd support/gradle && gradle buildNatives`, output under `build/os/mac_arm_64/`, copied beside the shipped `os/` directories). The executor first writes `needs-disassembly.txt` from E §9 (one symbol or address per line), re-runs the decompiler-dependent analyzers once (the stored analysis ran before the natives existed), then runs the committed Java post-script `DecompileListed.java` with `-noanalysis` to decompile the listed functions into the local `decomp/` directory. It answers, each with the evidence that shows it: the attributes fetched per element and the batching; the naming and pruning rules of the text; index assignment and re-resolution; the diff rules and when the cumulative or no-change forms are used; the click path and its activation decision; the typeText path (layout key codes or Unicode); paste and clipboard restore; the settle conditions and timings; intervention detection; the peer check (understanding only); and **the soft-link table**: the SipHash algorithm and the per-symbol salts the helper's `SoftLink` module uses (E §9 gives the method), and the names recovered by hashing the exported symbols of SkyLight, CoreGraphics, HIServices and AppKit (`dyld_info -exports` on the shared cache) against it, so the functions behind the click, key, focus-enforcer and batched-hierarchy paths are known by name; and the timing values and budgets E §10 could not see (settle, debounce, the diff line budget, array caps). The output is `F-helper-logic.md`, and from A, E and F the executor writes `sky-behaviour.md`, the consolidated behaviour specification the engine and renderer are built against, every rule tagged with its source. Static only, no execution of the helper.

**Spike.** `native/engine/` as a minimal Swift package: `ping`, `status`, `approve`, `shutdown`, `list_apps`, `resolve_app`, `get_app_state` (tree and screenshot) and `click`, `press_key`, `type_text` on routes 1–2, plus a minimal `--probe keystroke|input-source` (the seed of M2a's probe mode: probes (d) and (e) need an HID-state poster and a source switch, and the owner's Screen Sharing keystroke is (d)'s second poster; `measure.mjs` saves and restores the owner's source as the matrix will), with route 3 (fake activation, now fully named by `F`) as the second probe for keyboard actions into a background app and for clicks inside a web view; a stand-in renderer in the spike's service so the measured `getAXState` includes rendering. The spike runs the real facade and the real REPL host: `scripts/spike/engine/serve.mjs` spawns `src/repl-host/supervisor.mjs` directly with a hand-built environment (the computer surface enabled, the spike's service module registered as the trusted `sky` service, `CUA_ENGINE_APP` set), bypassing `replHostFor`'s macOS refusal without touching `src/`, so "in-cell" means what it means in the baseline: `performance.now()` around the facade call inside the kernel. `assemble.mjs` builds and signs the bundle on the MacBook with the owner's identity at a path the mini will serve as its approved engine (the seed of M1b's `buildEngine`); the scenes' fixed-state definitions, refusal checks and the two fixture launchers (Chrome for Testing, the Electron fixture app with its own bundle id) are `scripts/spike/engine/scenes.mjs` and `scripts/spike/engine/fixtures/`, and the device-side `FrontWindowProbe` is `scripts/spike/engine/front-window.swift`. The vendor side of every M0 measurement is produced by `measure.mjs --lane vendor`, which serves the approved vendor runtime through `scripts/native-baseline/prepare.mjs`'s approved-home path and drives the same four scenes through the vendor engine, since the baseline harness drives TextEdit only and gains its `--engine` option in M1b. Copied to the mini's approved location, launched through `open -n -g -a`, granted by the owner once at the console (the spike's `approve` step, `measure.mjs approve`: the engine's `approve` request raises the Accessibility and Screen Recording prompts and then, after the relaunch, the capture confirmation, all answered by the owner; on success `approved.json` is written). Measured on the mini inside a CI-opt hold, three runs each, against a fresh vendor triple of the same sitting (`measure.mjs --lane vendor`; the baseline harness gains `--engine` only in M1b): the baseline's own cells (`getAXState`, `getScreenshot`, `listApps`, the three-cell task, with `setup.activate`) on TextEdit, the tree read and bytes on the wire on Finder-200 and the Chrome loopback page, `valueMarkdown`'s cost, and the probes: (a) **activation**: for every scene family, does a pid-posted mouse-down or key event change the frontmost application or the key window (`FrontWindowProbe`); if yes, route 3 is implemented and probed the same way; (b) which route carries click and pressKey to each family; (c) does `open -a` without `-g` activate the engine, and do two copies of the bundle at different paths both come up under `open -n -g -a` and bind their own sockets; (c′) do the Chrome and Electron scenes expose their trees under the vendor's enablement rule (one attribute, never both), does the public per-element batched fetch meet the vendor's tree medians on Finder-200 and the Chrome page, and what does the private `_AXUIElementCopyHierarchy` route (named in `F` §11, `dlsym`) cost against it on the same states, which decides the engine's prefetch route; and what notification rate each scene produces at rest and after an action; (d) intervention: first whether the engine can create the vendor's listen-only annotated-session event tap with Accessibility granted and no Input Monitoring grant, whether its own pid-posted HID-source events reach that tap or reset the HID idle counter, and whether private-source key events land in TextEdit, Chrome for Testing and the Electron fixture at all (the three answers choose between the tap and the HID-idle fallback); then whether an HID-tap-posted probe event and a Screen Sharing keystroke are seen by the chosen mechanism; then the false-positive cases with no user input at all (`super+n`, a sheet opening and closing, the Open panel) must not fire; then the positive cases, a keystroke the owner sends over Screen Sharing into the driven app while it is frontmost, and a click into its background window; (e) the Korean and Unicode matrix on TextEdit and Chrome under both input sources, including a concealed `type_text` under the Korean source failing closed, and the per-context case: with the per-context input preference on and the driven app holding a Korean context in the background, whether keyed Latin events are transliterated to jamo; (f) **grants across copies**: with the owner at the console, whether a staged rebuild (new `build`, same path, same identity) and a copy at a new path keep the Accessibility grant and whether each re-raises the capture confirmation, and whether a running engine can observe its own Screen Recording grant (`CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` after the grant, before a relaunch), which neither the approve loop nor the in-session relaunch depends on but the install text should state. The finding-elements targets of part 5 are drawn from the recorded vendor text where it misses or leaves elements unnamed. Goldens: for each scene's fixed state, the vendor's text and the spike's raw `Skyshot` JSON (the Interfaces shape, not the stand-in renderer's text) on the same state, plus one action sequence per scene (the vendor's diff text and both engines' before-and-after trees), recorded in this sitting as the input of M1a's renderer and diff goldens; if M1a changes the `Skyshot` shape, the goldens are regenerated in an owner sitting on the mini, not invented. The spike adds the pinned `electron` dev dependency to `package.json`, since the fixture app is first built here. Development grants: the owner allowed the spike bundle to be granted on the MacBook (2026-10-18), at the MacBook's own approved location, so the tree walk, the screenshot and routes 1–2 are exercised on real scenes there before the mini sitting; the owner answers the MacBook's prompts as they would the mini's, and the measurements that count are still the mini's.

**Promote or discard.** Promote when, in one sitting against the fresh vendor triple, the spike's in-cell medians for `getAXState` (tree plus stand-in render) and `getScreenshot` on TextEdit are at or below the vendor's, and route 1, 2 or 3 carries click and pressKey to TextEdit and Finder with the frontmost application and topmost window unchanged. A screenshot miss is the same fork as a tree miss. If speed passes but no route carries background input to a family, the outcome is recorded per family and the design's route 4 applies to it; whether that is acceptable for the family is the owner's call, asked before M1a. If the tree or screenshot read cannot meet the vendor's medians within three tuning iterations in the sitting (batching, timeouts, the recovered private hierarchy call), the spike is discarded as a shape (the knowledge stays) and the fork, what the engine must do differently, goes to the owner, not the executor. Either way the design sections on input, settle, intervention and install are revised from the measurements, with a dated Decision Log entry, and the critique debate of Decision Log entry 1 runs on the revised design.

What it proves: nothing of the acceptance; its verdict goes to Surprises & Discoveries. What it does not touch: `src/`, the harnesses' selection logic, install (`package.json` gains only the `electron` dev dependency).

### M1a — Engine core, basic input, policy; goldens on hosted CI

What exists: `cua Engine.app` built from `native/engine/` with the socket server, peer check, the lock, protocol, app resolution and launch, the tree walk with lineage tokens and re-resolution (including elements owned by another process, the Open panel's), the service's numbering over the tokens, the screenshot at the vendor's size (the window's bounds × its screen's backing scale factor, each side rounded up; U1, Decision Log), `status` with `connections`, `approve`, and `click`, `press_key` and `type_text` on the routes M0 promoted, delivered to the element's owning process (with `concealed`); the service's client, renderer, diff and **policy with the forbidden list** in `src/services/engine/`, the approval memory in the supervisor, the `CUA_SKY_SERVICE` rename, the `concealed` mark in `src/services/sky.mjs`; the launch relay's `-n -g`; the seatbelt deny for the engine directory; the renderer and diff goldens from M0's captures, recorded after the vendor's sizing config (`406601636`) is re-read on the mini and still leaves legacy sizing off. Touches: `native/engine/`, `src/services/engine/`, `src/services/sky.mjs`, `src/services/secret-input.mjs`, `src/repl-host/launch-services.mjs`, `src/repl-host/supervisor.mjs`, `src/sandbox/seatbelt.mjs`, `src/runtime/launch.mjs` (the rename and the trusted code path only), the rename's other readers (`scripts/probe-runtime.mjs`, `scripts/probe/serve-fake-sky-target.mjs`, `scripts/probe/sky-wrapper-fixture.mjs`, `test/fixtures/linux-sky-service.mjs`, `test/runtime-*launch*.test.mjs`), `test/native-goldens/`, `test/services-*`, `.github/ci-kit.yml` (Swift build and unit tests), `docs/evidence/2026-10-18-hosted-macos-gate/baseline/` if the seatbelt change moves the fingerprint. Interfaces: produces `EngineProtocol`, `Skyshot`, `EngineClient`, `SkyshotRenderer`, `TreeDiff`, `Policy`, `ApprovalMemory`, `InputRoute`. Decisions: the renderer's rules from `sky-behaviour.md`, pinned by the goldens under part 3's rule; the lineage table as designed; `engineVersion` and `build` as defined. Does not touch: selection, attestation, the harnesses, install. Proves: part 7's goldens, policy and memory tests on hosted CI; the untrusted-engine path; nothing live.

### M1b — Build and approved location, selection and host switch, attestation, comparisons, scenes; live on the mini

Prerequisites on the mini, owner steps before the first live run: Xcode's Swift toolchain; the owner's Apple Development certificate in the login keychain (done 2026-10-18: the owner exported it from the MacBook and imported it on the mini; it validated only after Apple's WWDR G3 intermediate was added to the login keychain, since the mini's System keychain held only the expired G1, and the login keychain holds two copies of the identity, so `signingIdentity` is the SHA-1 of the one the MacBook also has, written to `~/.config/cua/engine.json`); `signingIdentity` written to `~/.config/cua/engine.json`; the `security set-key-partition-list` step (done 2026-10-19); the private key's access list trusting `codesign`, which the owner grants at the console by answering codesign's first key prompt, raised by `buildEngine`'s first GUI-domain sign, with the login password and Always Allow (automation never enters it; `security unlock-keychain` per SSH session is not the route, Decision Log 2026-10-20); then the first `cua engine approve` with the owner at the console. The grants are expected to read true already: the bundle keeps the spike's id `io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine` and the same identity, so its designated requirement is unchanged, and U8's same-path replacement kept all three grants with no confirmation; the by-hand steps run only for a grant that reads false. What exists: `buildEngine` (identity resolution from `CUA_SIGNING_IDENTITY` or the setting, with its refusal; build, staged assembly and signing (through the one-shot GUI-domain job when there is no GUI keychain, Install, signing and permissions), `shutdown` before the move into the approved location, `pending.json`, `--sign adhoc`) behind `cua engine build`, `cua engine approve` (the console loop that writes `approved.json`), `cua engine status` and `cua engine stop`; the harnesses consume the approved engine and never build it; `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE` resolved by the launcher per the selection table (default `vendor`) with the own-host rows working end to end; the attestation in the proxy; the three comparison modes, `--engine`, `--approved-engine`, the `build` check against the checkout, `setup.activate` after the bind and the engine-aware helper attestation in `scripts/native-baseline.mjs`; the scenes and the two fixture launchers promoted into the harness; the key guard comparing by pid; comparison A run on the mini. Touches: `src/runtime/engine.mjs` (new), `src/runtime/launch.mjs`, `src/cli.mjs` (`cua engine build|approve|status|stop`), `src/mcp/` (the `_meta` attestation), `scripts/native-baseline.mjs` and `scripts/native-baseline/*` (`compare.mjs`'s comparable set and modes, `macos.mjs`, `remote.mjs`, `prepare.mjs`), `scripts/repl-parity/selection.mjs`, `scripts/repl-parity/scenes-mac.mjs`, `scripts/native-matrix/fixtures/`, `scripts/accept/key-guard.mjs` (pid comparison), `scripts/accept/front-window.swift`, `test/runtime-launch.test.mjs`. Interfaces: produces `EngineSelection`, `EngineAttestation`, `EngineInstall.buildEngine|approveEngine|engineStatus|stopEngine|installedEngine`, `NativeMacScenes`, `FrontWindowProbe`; consumes M1a's. Decisions: the selection table as written, including the `engine_not_installed` and no-Node rows; the approved location and the approve step; harnesses never install or build their own engine. Does not touch: the remaining methods, settle, intervention, install integration, docs. Proves: comparison A's full default plan within the no-regression rule (the observation rows of acceptance 5 and the first `task.*` rows, diagnostic for the settle claim until M2a), the selection table live. **Finder's golden is compared as full text here**, which retires its `redaction` and `engine or renderer` declarations (M1a.3): the Finder scene's fixed state hides the sidebar so the window carries only run-owned content, or the redactor keeps the AX vocabulary (identifier prefixes, custom actions' names, action descriptions), which is not owner content. **The goldens' corrections expire here** (the coordinating session's ruling, 2026-10-19): M1b re-records M1a's renderer goldens live from the M1a.2b engine on the scene states, and deletes every engine-fault correction `test/native-goldens/expected.json` declares; a correction still needed then is an engine defect to fix, not a declaration to keep.

### M2a — The full method set, settle, intervention

What exists: all 11 methods on cua's engine with the input routes as M0 measured, out-of-process targets, settle with its cap, intervention, the post-intervention lock, lock screen, paste with the lazy provider and restore, the `--probe` mode (`keystroke`, `input-source`, `focus`, `watch-activations`). Touches: `native/engine/`, `src/services/engine/` and `src/services/sky.mjs` (method mapping, error sentences, `cua/inputRoute`, `cua/settle`, `cua/actionInterval`, `cua/receivedAt`, `cua/hidIdleMs`, `cua/serviceMs`, `cua/engineMs`). Interfaces: produces `UserProbe`; consumes M1a's. Decisions: the route table as the Input section states (keys by route 2′, synthesized clicks by route 3 with the activation per action and the activation click skipped outside the window's frame, the clicks' ladder 1 → 3 → 4, route 2 never a default, `activationFailed` on a failed guard), route 4 always reported and reachable only by the per-app override; the enablement re-check and release; the FIFO paste lock; the settle rule as designed, with the vendor's order and timing; the debounce rule for `get_app_state`; the intervention mechanism the listen-only tap under Input Monitoring, with detection off and reported when the grant is missing (the owner's decision of 2026-10-20); from M1a (Decision Log, M1a.2 and M1a.2b): the menu-bar item's activate-and-record path (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.8) built with the observation of the recorded menu, replacing M1a's `menuBarItemUnsupported`; scroll-into-view before a synthesized element click (the vendor's `positionElement`); `performSecondaryAction` matched as the vendor matches it, by the action's description against the element's live, unfiltered actions plus the Expand/Collapse pseudo-actions (revised 2026-10-21 from the vendor reading, Decision Log "M2a vendor reading"; the earlier text had it validated by `filterActions`, which the vendor does not do). Does not touch: the harnesses, Linux, install, docs. Proves: part 7's unit tests for the new paths on hosted CI; comparison A's `task.*` rows now authoritative on the mini (run under M1b's harness).

### M2b — The matrix, oracles and the `native-mac` suite

What exists: `scripts/native-matrix.mjs` (the method matrix, per-scene speed cells, the finding-elements scorer, the non-interference oracle with the device-side front-window probe, the activation watcher and the key guard's user-window case, the concurrent-use cells through the probe mode with their interval check, the two positive intervention cells, the focus check, the Korean and Unicode matrix with input-source and per-context preference save and restore, the settle cell, and `summary`), taking `--device`, `--host`, `--engine`, `--approved-runtime` and `--approved-engine` and reusing `prepare.mjs`'s admission and state moves; the `native-mac` suite, `--engine`, `--approved-runtime`, `--approved-engine`, a darwin native command and the modulo-renumbering diff with its `summary` line in the parity harness (today's native lane is Linux-only, `scripts/repl-parity/native.mjs`, and `prepare` installs a fresh home, which on macOS must serve the approved runtime and engine instead). Touches: `scripts/native-matrix.mjs` (new) and `scripts/native-matrix/*`, `scripts/repl-parity.mjs` and `scripts/repl-parity/*` (`parseSelection`, `registry.mjs`, `native.mjs`, `prepare-device.mjs`, the expected file), `scripts/accept/key-guard.mjs` (the user-window case), `test/repl-parity/scenarios/`. Interfaces: produces `NativeMatrix`; consumes M1b's and M2a's. Decisions: the user-window case of the key guard identified by pid and bounds; the fixture app's three controls. Does not touch: `src/`. Proves: acceptance 3, 4 and 7's rows on the mini; the differential suite's first full run (acceptance 6's form).

### M3 — Engine-aware tooling, comparisons B and C, Linux approvals

What exists: `doctor`, `verify.mjs` (`--engine`, `--bind` with approval observation, the `cua/nativeEngine` check), `scripts/accept/textedit.mjs`, `scripts/accept-native.mjs` (`--engine`; items 12 and 13 run pinned to the vendor engine while it exists), `scripts/probe-lifecycle.mjs` and `scripts/probe/lib.mjs` engine-aware (the socket holders, the approval observation by behaviour); comparisons B and C running; `CUA_SHIM_PERSIST=always` refused; the Linux approvals through the policy module with `src/mcp/surface.mjs`'s Linux rule revised and the Linux parity lane answering the pinned request instead of declining. Touches: `src/runtime/doctor.mjs`, `src/mcp/server.mjs`, `src/mcp/surface.mjs`, `verify.mjs`, `scripts/accept/*`, `scripts/accept-native.mjs`, `scripts/probe-lifecycle.mjs`, `scripts/probe/lib.mjs`, `src/services/sky.mjs` (Linux through the policy), `scripts/repl-parity/*` (Linux approvals), `test/mcp-*`. Interfaces: consumes M1b's and M2b's; produces nothing new. Decisions: the Linux method list and keys as the service section states. Does not touch: install and signing, docs beyond the surface rules. Proves: acceptance 2, 6 and 9; comparisons B and C; `accept-native` items 7 and 8 on cua's engine; the Linux parity suite with approvals at 0 unexpected differences.

### M4 — Install, signing, permissions, docs

What exists: `cua install` integrating `buildEngine` under the selection rule, `--engine` and `--engine-sign adhoc` (identity resolution and its refusal are M1b's), reading the sign wait as `cua engine build` does (`--sign-wait`, `CUA_SIGN_WAIT_MS`; M1b.1's entry); the permission prompts and the relaunch-after-grant behaviour; `accept-native` item 9's fresh-onboarding check split into its own item and runnable on the mini (owner-run revocation step marked); `README.md`, `docs/onboarding-new-mac.md` (identity setup, the SSH keychain step, the approved engine) and `src/mcp/surface.mjs`'s macOS host rules revised (typeText under each input source, the secrets rule, the engine's name in System Settings); the hosted gate builds the real bundle; `prepare.mjs` and `hosted-gate.sh` opting in where they need the engine. Touches: `src/runtime/install.mjs`, `src/runtime/engine.mjs`, `src/cli.mjs`, `scripts/accept-native.mjs`, `scripts/native-baseline/prepare.mjs`, `scripts/sandbox-probe/hosted-gate.sh`, `.github/ci-kit.yml`, docs. Interfaces: completes `EngineInstall`. Decisions: install leaves the engine alone unless selected or asked; a default-selected build failure does not fail the install; the relaunch after a `permissionsPending` screenshot waits for the engine to have no other connection. Does not touch: the default selection. Proves: acceptance 1 and 8.

### M5 — Acceptance and the default switch

What exists: the whole verification system run on the mini in one sitting, three attested runs per side, the three-point report under `docs/evidence/<sitting date>-own-native-engine/`, the owner's own sitting (the owner drives apps of their choice on both engines from Claude Code and judges breadth; the plugin's hook answering the approval is observed here), the owner's decisions on any missed 10% beat and on any route-4 family, then the default `cua` in the launcher with the `engine_not_installed` warn row, the plugin bumped, the first-call document checked for stale claims. Touches: `src/runtime/launch.mjs` (default), `docs/evidence/`, the spec, `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`. Proves: acceptance 1–9 as written, with the commands under Concrete Steps.

### M6 — Retirement

After a soak the owner ends (the criterion: no `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=vendor` rollback needed in the owner's use since M5), the vendor side leaves macOS as described under "Engine selection, host switch and retirement", including cua's own module tree; the harnesses' vendor lanes are removed on macOS (the Linux lanes stay until #171); `accept-native` items 12 and 13 are retired; manifests, `checks.mjs`, `doctor.mjs` and tests lose the macOS vendor rows; the default-without-engine and no-Node rows become refusals and `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=vendor` answers `engine_retired`; README's licence note says the native engine is cua's own with the derivation note #120 needs; acceptance 1–4, 8 (without 12 and 13) and 9 (the retirement sentence) and `npm test` are run again beside 10. Touches: `src/runtime/install.mjs`, `manifest.mjs`, `checks.mjs`, `doctor.mjs`, `launch.mjs`, `runtime/modules/` (new), `package.json` (`classic-level`), `scripts/native-baseline/*`, `scripts/repl-parity/*`, `scripts/accept-native.mjs`, `scripts/accept/*`, tests, docs. Interfaces: produces `ModuleTree`. Proves: acceptance 10, and 1–4, 8, 9 repeated. #171 opens.

## Interfaces and Dependencies

**EngineProtocol** (`native/engine/Sources/Engine/Protocol.swift`, `src/services/engine/protocol.mjs`): JSON-RPC 2.0 over the 4-byte little-endian length-prefixed framing, 8 MiB per frame; methods `ping`, `execute`, `status`, `approve`, `shutdown`; `serverApiVersion = "cua-engine-1"`.

```
ping     {clientApiVersion: string} → {serverApiVersion: string, engineVersion: string, build: string}
execute  {method: string, args: object, deadlineUnixMilliseconds: number, lineage?: string} → result | error
         (lineage: the lineage an element token in args belongs to; a token from another lineage or a retired one answers the
          "no longer valid" sentence; the deadline is checked on receipt and cancels a settle or launch wait still running when it
          passes. An element-targeted method carries args.element: token, the service's translation of the facade's element_index)
status   {} → {engineVersion, build, accessibility: boolean, screenRecording: boolean, inputMonitoring: boolean, canPostHid: boolean, socket: string, pid: number, bundlePath: string, connections: number}
         (connections = open connections other than the requesting one, so `connections > 0` means "someone else"; shutdown's "busy" uses the same count;
          inputMonitoring is the non-prompting listen preflight, CGPreflightListenEventAccess, the intervention tap's grant, read for doctor and
          the approve step and never requested by the engine; false means the engine runs with intervention detection off)
approve  {} → {accessibility: boolean, screenRecording: boolean, captured: boolean}   (calls the two prompting requests, whose prompts may not appear, and reads the grants;
          captures one display image and discards it once both read true; the by-hand path is the command's, "Install, signing and permissions")
shutdown {} → {} then exit, or error "busy" {connections: n} while another connection is open

methods: list_apps, get_app_state, click, drag, paste, perform_secondary_action, press_key, scroll, select_text,
         set_value, type_text (the facade's shapes, R/A-contract.md §1.4), resolve_app {app} | {pid}
         (a coordinate in click, drag or scroll is in the pixel space of the screenshot the connection's last screenshot-bearing
          observation of the app returned, divided by that observation's per-axis scale and offset by the current window origin;
          refused as noScreenshotForCoordinates, nothing posted, when the connection holds no such observation, the named lineage
          is not current, or the app's primary window is no longer the one captured; "Screenshot")

resolve_app → {appPath: string, bundleIdentifier: string, displayName: string, isRunning: boolean, pid: number | null}
get_app_state {app, disableDiff?: boolean, includeScreenshot?: boolean, outputDirectory: string} → Skyshot
Skyshot  {app: {bundleIdentifier, displayName, appPath, pid, catalyst: boolean}, window: {title: string | null, frame: Rect, pid: number},
          lineage: string, revision: number, nodes: Node[], truncated: {depth?: number, count?: number} | null,
          screenshot: {url: string, width: number, height: number, scale: number} | null, focused: string | null,
          selectedText: string | null, selectedElements: string[], menuBar: Node[], settle?: "capped"}
          (app.catalyst: the vendor's ApplicationUIElement.isCatalystApp 0x100704c88, the application's
          `_AXExhibitsMacCatalystBehavior`, false when absent; filterActions drops AXCancel in such an app (M1a.2b).
          window.title: the vendor's `ApplicationWindow.title` 0x10076e174, the window server's name for the window, else its
          AXTitle, "Desktop" for an untitled Finder window at the desktop-icon level, null when there is none (the renderer
          prints `Unknown`); without Screen Recording the window server gives no name and the AX title stands (M1a.2b).
          menuBar: the AXMenuBar element at depth 0 (roleDescription null when it lists none, printed `menu bar`), then its
          top-level items at depth 1 but the first (the Apple menu) and any titled `Debug`, each read through the attributes
          it lists, tokens in the same lineage after the window's nodes (`addMenuBarWithImmediateItems` 0x1007569b8); empty
          when the bar itself cannot be read. The renderer prints the items through the ordinary line with their actions
          cleared, as the vendor does, and synthesizes no bar of its own (M1a.2b); their order in
          the text is the renderer's, from sky-behaviour.md. focused and selectedElements: the tokens the focused/selected-text
          trailer names. settle: "capped" when the settle that preceded this observation hit the 5 s cap — on the
          observation result, since the settle runs at the next get_app_state, not inside the action, R/sky-behaviour.md §23.
          timings, the engine's diagnostics, carries focusRead when the focus read named no node: the AXError, "notAnElement"
          or "outsideTheWalk"; M1a.2b)
Node     {token: string, depth: number, pid: number, role: string, subrole: string | null, roleDescription: string | null,
          title: string | null, description: string | null, value: string | number | boolean | null,
          valueType: "float" | "integer" | "boolean" | "string" | "date" | "unknown" | null, valueMarkdown: string | null,
          identifier: string | null, actionDescriptions: (string | null)[],
          valueDescription?: string, placeholder?: string, numberOfCharacters?: number, help?: string, url?: string,
          traits?: number, servesAsTitleFor?: string[], rowIndexRange?: [number, number], columnIndexRange?: [number, number],
          domClassList?: string[], scrollBars?: {horizontal: boolean, vertical: boolean},
          flags: {enabled: boolean, focused: boolean, focusable: boolean, selected: boolean, selectable: boolean,
                  settable: boolean, expanded?: boolean, disclosable?: boolean},
          actions: string[], frame: Rect | null, children: number,
          shown?: {count: number, of: number, subset: "visible" | "omitted", ranges?: [number, number][]}}   (the width rule: present when an
          element's children were cut to the visible subset or omitted above 499; the renderer's grammar needs every
          named field, R/sky-behaviour.md §2.4 and §23 — token is the engine's per-lineage handle token, stable while the handle is
          CFEqual at a matched position and retired after one absent revision; the service numbers the tokens that survive its
          transformations, so the model's indexes are the service's ("Indexes, tokens and handles"; M0's raw Skyshot goldens carry
          the spike engine's indexes and are re-keyed when M1a records the renderer goldens); servesAsTitleFor names the tokens a
          title element serves (the title-association rule), shown.ranges the index ranges of the children kept (the vendor's
          `(showing lo-hi, … of N)`), valueType backs the `settable, <type>` flag, domClassList
          backs the DOM-origin markers, disclosable/disclosing pairs with expanded for outline rows; the optional
          attributes are present only when the element carries them, so a tree from either fetch route has the same
          shape. The grammar never prints `checked`, so the flag is dropped. The derivations are the vendor's (FUN_10074cb90 in
          get_app_state's fullUIContext; `Derivations.swift`, M1a.2b): valueType is `valueTypeName` FUN_10074a794 (the CFNumber's
          own float or integer type; boolean for a switch or toggle checkbox), null without a value; actionDescriptions runs
          parallel to actions, the vendor's description past its fixed table and a custom action's `Name:` (FUN_1007816a0):
          null for an action either describes (the renderer prints the table's English word or the name, in every language;
          the system is not asked), else the system's description (AXUIElementCopyActionDescription), else the action's
          name; url is
          AXURL, or for a window without one its AXDocument parsed as a URL; flags.disclosable is AXDisclosing settable;
          flags.selected is AXSelected, else the selected trait of AXTraits; flags.selectable is AXSelected settable, never on a menu
          or menu-bar item (FUN_10077d1e4; AXSelectable is not read); flags.settable is read only for a node with a value and, in a Chromium window, only for AXComboBox,
          AXIncrementor (not a date or time field's), AXTextArea, AXTextField and a tab, FUN_100751614; roleDescription is null
          when the element lists none; scrollBars, present on a node with a page-scroll action, says whether it answers
          AXHorizontalScrollBar and AXVerticalScrollBar. `actions` are the element's own; the renderer applies the vendor's
          filterActions, FUN_100782c24, to what it prints)
input results → {meta: {inputRoute: 1 | "2'" | 2 | 3 | 4 | "paste", startedAt: number, endedAt: number,
                        receivedAt: number, hidIdleMs: number | null, entered?: number, routeAdvice?: string, intervention?: "off",
                        activationGuardMs?: number, pointUnconfirmed?: true}}
                (every input method; the four times are Unix ms from the engine's clock; "2'" is route 2′, the plain post, the route of
                 every key press and typed text; 3 is every synthesized click; 2 appears only when a spike cell forces it; entered
                 is a type_text's UTF-16 units entered, on its result and on an error that stopped it; routeAdvice names the per-app
                 route-4 override after route 3's guard has failed three actions in a row for the app; intervention: "off" when the
                 Input Monitoring grant is missing and the tap is not running, so the stop, the refusal and the lock are not in force)
input errors  → the error below plus the same meta (startedAt/endedAt null when nothing was posted), for userIntervened in both its
                sentences (the pre-action "still interacting" refusal and the in-sequence "user changed"), concealedInputUnavailable,
                inputSourceChanged, noScreenshotForCoordinates and activationFailed (route 3's guard failed: the user's app lost
                frontmost or the 1 s poll timed out; the click is refused, nothing posted, never retried with ⌘); every error of an
                input method carries the meta, startedAt/endedAt
                null when nothing was posted (an unresolvable or forbidden app, or a forbidden owning process of the target element,
                refused at dispatch included)
type_text/paste/set_value args may carry concealed: true (set by src/services/sky.mjs on a substituted request);
  a concealed request that would need the paste route fails with errorName "concealedInputUnavailable" before input
error    {code: number (the vendor's codes where mapped; -10005 otherwise), message: string, errorName: string}
  engine-only errorNames the wrapper admits: concealedInputUnavailable, inputSourceChanged, noScreenshotForCoordinates, activationFailed (all value-free)
  engine-only errorNames the wrapper does not admit (no secret is substituted into a click): menuBarItemUnsupported (a click on a menu-bar
    item or on the menu bar's own node, refused before anything is posted until M2a's activate-and-record path; M1a.2; its sentence names the app's keyboard
    shortcut through pressKey as the alternative, M1a.2b)
  activationGuardMs: route 3's guard time, present only when a guard ran; pointUnconfirmed: true when the hit-test at a synthesized element
    point answered nothing or only a container (M1a.2)
  noScreenshotForCoordinates: a coordinate click, drag or scroll with no usable screenshot on the connection ("Screenshot"); nothing
    posted, the input meta carried with startedAt/endedAt null; the sentence names getScreenshot as the way to get an image to click
    in (c13759a4's "There is no screenshot of the app's window to place the coordinates in. Try to get the on-screen content again
    and see if that resolves the issue." names the tree instead and is revised in M1a)
```
