The engine now does the same for the first two cases (`preparationOpen`, Skyshot.swift); live, the retry binds in about 1 s (00:39Z). It is not an activation, so the Design's rule holds, and the Design now names the reopen because it is visible.
     Alternative: the engine's route-3 fake activation to the windowless app (maws-3f's fallback had the vendor activated). Lost because the vendor's code shows a reopen, not an activation.
  2. **The vendor's reopen of an app whose primary window is minimized is not taken**: the reopen un-minimizes the owner's window, a visible change an observation should not make. A declared difference from the vendor; a scene that needs it (an app observed while minimized) reopens the question.
  2b. **Three more boundaries, from the fix's review (maws-3f's rulings, the second fix commit):** a primary-window read that *failed* (the app could not answer, e.g. `kAXErrorCannotComplete`) is not an absence: the engine never opens such an app, retries the read until the request's deadline and then answers `timeoutReached` — a declared difference, since the vendor's failed read reads as no window and reopens (cua does not act on an app it could not read); the preparation's reads are charged to the request's deadline and no open is sent once it has passed; and the open's own process is the one observed after it, so an app replaced between resolution and the open is followed rather than its dead pid polled (`prepareTarget`, `readPrimaryWindow`).
  3. **The harness's three fixes**: Finder's absent display keys are a saved state of their own, read as macOS's default and restored by deletion (macOS 27 words the missing key differently from 26, which the read did not recognise); Chrome for Testing gets a fresh profile directory on every launch, a restart included, since a restart on the same profile came up with the AI-mode omnibox placeholder (the eligibility answer the omnibox stores in the profile; Chrome for Testing is unbranded and fetches no variations seed, so no switch applies); and the baseline's bind check allows 1 s of `ps` etime slack, since etime subtracts whole seconds and read 10 s for the TextEdit the run's own bind had launched. The after-hold review then made ownership independent of that rounding: the run re-reads TextEdit's presence immediately before the bind and refuses one already running, the age check staying as a secondary guard (24c1f01f); and a Chrome scene that fails to start removes its fresh profile once its processes have stopped, or records it for `down` (b8ec2d76).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b closing wave, 2026-10-21, maws-3f's instructions after the hold; 22c92a99, 2acf166b, 0281bb97, 875631b6, 32036cb5): the harness changes that let the remaining two goldens record, done off the mini before the closing hold.
  1. **Finder's unset chrome defaults follow the running macOS's major version** (`sw_vers -productVersion`): the status bar is shown unset on 27 and hidden on 26; the sidebar is shown and the path bar hidden on both. Setup and restore both read the running OS's default, so a macOS update between a dead run's save and a later restore toggles the Finder that runs now; the saved record's version is informational. An unknown or unreadable version refuses `up finder` before any window opens; the key writes need no default, so `down` and `restore-finder` still put every key back, an unset one by deletion. Unconfirmed on a device: the sidebar's unset default on 27 (the mini sets `ShowSidebar=1`) and the three defaults on 26.
  2. **Every golden observation is a settled read; Chrome's fixed state is the filled page.** The sitting's Chrome mismatch was the page's accessibility values still filling (the heading level, checkbox and radio states empty in the kept state, filled on every restart), which both engines read alike: the engine's Chromium enablement is the vendor's (AXManualAccessibility, then a wait for the web area's presence, not its contents; F §9, `FUN_1006f1670`), so the fix is the harness's (maws-3f's ruling), and whether `get_app_state` settles on content stays M2a's question. A settled read takes full observations 500 ms apart until two in a row pass the scene's checks with one digest, within 10 s, each request given the remaining budget as its deadline and no answer accepted past it; Chrome's checks refuse `page_filling` until its web area shows the filled values, since the unfilled page can hold still past 500 ms. The first fixed-state read and the start of cua's action read through a wrong window or a filling page; the two reads after a vendor capture is written (`verify`) refuse either at once, so an owner's window seen there invalidates the capture rather than being outwaited (the wave's review, P1). The controller resets the scene on `start_state`, `unstable` and `page_filling`. Applied to every scene: Electron is Chromium and a Finder toggle can still be animating.
     Alternative: CDP's load event alone (the coordinator's first direction). Lost because the DOM was long loaded when the values were still empty.
  3. **`up chrome` waits for the page's own report of `load`** (a `sendBeacon` to the run's loopback page server, `connect-src 'self'`), up to 15 s, with the failed-up cleanup unchanged. Chosen over a CDP port, which any local process could use to drive the run's Chrome.
  1b. **Item 1 withdrawn by the 03:53Z hold (2026-10-21; 889ce621, 35c40e2a, fb8d073b, ce5b0669): Finder's chrome is read off its window, not predicted from its keys.** The hold recorded TextEdit, Chrome and Electron at the first attempt (`m1b/goldens-run-incomplete-84c6eeea/`) and Finder refused three times. Read in the run's own window by structure: with `ShowStatusBar` unset the window draws the status bar, yet the first ⌘/ wrote 1 with nothing changing and only the second hid it (Finder's toggle state and its window disagreed; the path bar's and sidebar's agreed); and a `defaults write` before the window opened changed nothing, since Finder reads its chrome keys only at launch. So setup observes the run's first window before any toggle and saves the owner's visible state beside the keys (the first record wins, so a dead run's next `up` keeps the owner's), then toggles each part only while the window still shows it, at most twice, refusing otherwise; teardown toggles each part until the window matches the saved visible state, then writes the keys back as before. The detectors are structural (`#statusBar`, an AXList outside the list for the path bar, an outline besides the list for the sidebar), never text. The review added two recovery rules: the restored marker is dropped before a toggle's key is sent, and a saved record that cannot say what the windows showed (no schema, or no visible state with keys moved) is refused by `up` and restored keys-only by `restore-finder`, which says the visible chrome needs the View menu. The version table and its `sw_vers` read are gone. maws-3f's design (the observation-driven teardown is theirs: replaying the toggles cannot restore an unset key).
  1c. **The goldens' last hold (04:52Z, 0b44493e) and the vendor's capped item count (maws-3f's ruling, 2026-10-21).** All four scenes recorded at the first attempt (`m1b/goldens-final/`); the first pass refused Finder only because the recorder compared window frames by their JSON text, whose key order the engine varies (0b44493e compares the four numbers). The expiry audit deleted 23 of the 26 corrections (the 3 left are TextEdit scene-state declarations); its one `ENGINE DEFECT`, TextEdit's `windowUrl`, was an expired correction pinning M0's path, which the audit misread because it counted a removal that only removes differences as a need (fixed in the close wave). What remained is one line in Finder's fixed-state and action-before goldens: the vendor renders the list as `(showing 0-21 of 100 items)` where the list holds 205 and cua renders `of 205 items`. The vendor's N is `childCount` (`availableSubsetDescription` 0x10063e220 through `get_childCount` 0x10079e94c) over a tree `_AXUIElementCopyHierarchy` prefetched with AXChildren capped at MaxArrayCount 100 (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §1.1), so it never exceeds 100. **Declared as a vendor defect, scoped to N alone** (the line otherwise equal, the ranges equal, the vendor's N exactly min(cua's N, 100), cua's N above 100); the engine and the Skyshot keep the true count.
     Alternative: the renderer prints `min(N, 100)`, which is parity in text with no declaration and no rebuild. Lost because the vendor's number is wrong: it tells the model a 205-item list ends at 100, so a model looking for item 150 would believe it is not there, and the owner's rulings ask for parity with the vendor and for beating it at finding elements; reproducing a wrong count on purpose serves the first against the second. M1b's expiry rule is for engine-fault corrections, and this is the vendor's fault, declared as part 3 declares expected differences.
  1d. **The recorder's put-back gains the owner's front app, and its meta Finder's chrome record (the close wave, b5923385, 013f4eaa; maws-3f).** After the 04:52Z hold Finder was frontmost where Terminal had been: every scene activates its app. `record` now reads the front app before anything is prepared and re-activates it at the end when the same process still runs it and it is not a scene app (`cleanup` likewise, from preparation.json); meta.json keeps its bundle id, never its pid. meta.json also carries Finder's saved visible state and its setup and teardown toggle counts (booleans and counts only); the 04:52Z recording predates both, so its Finder evidence is the first-attempt pass, `frames.same`, and the window observed after teardown.
  4. **AutoStop's tests arm deadlines that cannot race the next line** (a 0 s deadline fired before `cancel()` on a loaded hosted runner, ci-gate 39531662385).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (landing, 2026-10-21, maws-3f): **M0, M1a and M1b land on main together, as one squash-merged PR**, after M1b closed. This departs from the Plan of Work's "each milestone lands as its own PR". The reviews that rule asks for ran: M1a's whole-branch reviewer-high (a807827a..bd1136f1), and reviewer-high on M1b (b2ccdc3b..84c6eeea), which found no material defect. The delta to 702b04d9 changes only harness, tests, goldens and docs, and its reviews were reviewer-medium on the Finder fix and reviewer-low on the close wave, both correct after their fixes. From M2a on, each milestone again lands as its own PR, on a branch cut from main.
  Rationale: M1a's goldens carried engine-fault corrections that only M1b's live recording could expire. A main holding M1a alone would have pinned corrections the design said were temporary. Squash also keeps the `native/engine/.build` blobs that 3fcc4e49 committed (about 217 MB, untracked in ccbb8242) out of main's history.
  Alternative: rewrite the branch to drop `.build` from history and land with a merge commit, keeping the per-commit history on main. Lost because the rewrite would change every commit hash the spec, the reports and the evidence cite. Those commits stay reachable through the PR's head ref (`refs/pull/<n>/head`) after the branch is deleted.
- Decision (M2a pre-flight, 2026-10-21, on branch `feat/117-m2a` from main 5941e988): M2a runs as five tasks (Progress). M2a.1 → M2a.2 → M2a.3 run in this worktree one after another, since all three touch the input path (`Input.swift`, `Methods.swift`, `Routes.swift`); M2a.4 touches the observation path and the wrapper and runs beside them in its own worktree once the vendor reading below lands; M2a.5 is the controller's, inside a CI-opt hold maws-3f grants, on the build the whole-milestone review has passed. Gaps settled in the spirit of the design:
  1. **Two questions carried from M1b are read in the vendor's code before M2a.4 builds them**: whether the vendor settles a `get_app_state` that no action preceded (its UI-settle wait `0x1006f7408` and every caller on the observation path, the preparation's launch and reopen included), and how its primary-window selection and capture treat a minimized window. The vendor's rule is adopted for the first; a deviation is its own entry.
  2. **The minimized primary window** (M1b.5 item 2 left it waiting out 5 s and failing): the engine never un-minimizes the owner's window. It observes a minimized primary window in place when the Accessibility API exposes its tree, **with no screenshot** (`screenshot: null`, the reason in the result's meta): a minimized window is not composited, so any off-screen capture is at best its last frame before minimizing, which no longer shows the window's state the model would act on (maws-3f, 2026-10-21); a request that cannot be served without the window on screen (a synthesized click or drag, a coordinate) is refused with a sentence naming the minimized window as the cause, while the Accessibility routes (an advertised action, `set_value`, `select_text`) still act. Where the tree itself is not exposed, the observation is refused with that sentence rather than timing out. The vendor's reopen of an app whose primary window is minimized (it un-minimizes the owner's window) is declared as an expected difference. M2a.4 builds it and M2a.5 checks it live on the TextEdit scene's own window.
  3. **The spike's `probe_tap` and `probe_spike` methods leave the engine** with M2a.1, as the M1a pre-flight entry scheduled; `scripts/spike/engine/` stays M0's record. `spikeRoute`, the forced-route tool, stays.
  4. **`--probe input-source` must select an input mode** (Korean 2-Set, `com.apple.inputmethod.Korean.2SetKorean`) as well as a keyboard layout and read it back, since part 6's `pressKey` row under each source (M2b) is driven by it (Surprises, MAWS P4-B2); M2a.5 checks the selection and the restore to ABC live, reading the per-context input preference before the switch and restoring it with the source.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2a vendor reading, 2026-10-21; the questions of the M2a pre-flight entry, read from the decompile with three helper readers and re-checked in the disassembly; the local record is the workspace's `m2a-vendor-reading.md`, folded into `R/F-helper-logic.md` and `R/sky-behaviour.md` by M2a.4). Each ruling adopts the vendor's rule unless it says otherwise:
  1. **First-observation settle.** The vendor settles an observation that no action preceded in one case only: its preparation opened the app (a launch, or the non-activating reopen of an app whose primary window is missing or minimized); then `waitUntilHasPrimaryWindow(5.0, 0.05)` and `waitForUIToSettle(delay 0.05, notificationDelay 0.25, scroll events off)` at `0x100164c08`, the settle's tree discarded. Otherwise `needsUISettleBeforeSkyshot` starts false (`0x1000181f8`) and the first observation captures at once, web-shaped or not, and nothing on the observation path waits for web content to fill (the web-area wait checks presence; the busy walk runs only inside a settle). **Adopted** (the Settle paragraph): the engine settles after its own launch or reopen, never otherwise, and adds no fill wait; a scene state that must be filled is the harness's to wait for, as M1b's settled read does. F §8.1 and §8.6 missed this site.
  2. **Minimized windows.** The vendor's primary-window selection never filters `AXMinimized`; only the preparation reads it, reopening an app whose primary window reads minimized, and the un-minimize is AppKit's reopen default (inference); every `get_app_state` runs the preparation, so the vendor effectively never observes a minimized window, and actions never reopen. Its capture takes an off-screen `SCWindow` by window id through SkyLight (what a minimized window's image holds is undetermined). With Stage Manager on, every vendor observation writes `AXMinimized = false` on an off-stage window and may perform `AXAddToStage`, its only `AXMinimized` write. **cua's rule is the pre-flight entry's item 2 unchanged**, and two expected differences are declared: the vendor's reopen of a minimized app, and its Stage Manager writes (cua never writes `AXMinimized` or performs `AXAddToStage`; an off-stage window that reads minimized falls under the minimized rule).
  3. **`perform_secondary_action`** matches the action's description (`Increment`, the word the renderer prints), exactly, first hit, against the element's live, **unfiltered** actions plus the pseudo-actions `SAIExpand` and `SAICollapse`, which toggle `AXDisclosing`; an unknown action answers `<action> is not a valid secondary action for <element>` with `<element>` the decimal element index. **Adopted**; this supersedes the M1a.2b entry's item 5 sentence that M2a validates by `filterActions` and the Plan's M2a text, revised.
  4. **`set_value`** checks settability, focuses the field (`AXFocused`, with a synthesized-click fallback), coerces the string to the current value's type (numbers through an `en_US` `NSNumberFormatter`, Booleans from `on`/`off` or `boolValue`, a switch or toggle checkbox forced to Boolean), writes, and re-fetches without reading the value back; the search-field auto-submit (a synthesized Return) is off for `node_repl`-class clients, cua's kind. **Adopted**, no auto-submit.
  5. **`select_text`** searches the rendered Markdown value first, then the attributed or plain value; case-sensitive, `prefix` and `suffix` immediately adjacent, exactly one match required; Markdown syntax at a match's edge skipped inward; UTF-16 offsets; `selection_type` `text`, `cursor_before`, `cursor_after` giving `(L, N)`, `(L, 0)`, `(L + N, 0)`; settability checked first, then the field focused as in 4. **Adopted.**
  6. **`scroll`** is one wheel event in pixels, `round(pages × max(element or window length, 100))`, `wheelCount` 1 with the horizontal delta in the second wheel, no search for a scroll area. **Adopted, one difference declared**: cua posts a horizontal scroll with two wheels so its delta is read (the vendor's may not scroll horizontally; M2b's matrix checks: if the vendor's one-wheel horizontal scroll moves nothing, it is declared a vendor defect as Finder's capped item count is, otherwise the two are equivalent and nothing is declared; maws-3f, 2026-10-21).
  7. **`drag`**: down, three dragged events (start, midpoint, end) each with its own event number, up, all aimed at the start point's window; the vendor ⌘-flags them. **Adopted with the route table's difference**: a drag into a background app takes route 3's activation like a synthesized click, never ⌘.
  8. **Element middle click**: the vendor turns it into a left click. **Not adopted**: cua synthesizes a middle click at the element's point by route 3, since a middle click means something else (a new tab on a link); a declared difference. Route 3's activation must then deliver no left-button event at the element's point: only the middle button's down and up land there, pinned by a unit test (maws-3f, 2026-10-21).
  9. **`positionElement`** (scroll into view): `AXScrollToVisible` (the action, or the attribute on cells), else the scroll bars' values, else `AXMakeScreenRectVisible`, then a poll of up to 0.5 s every 0.1 s whose timeout is swallowed; before a synthesized element click, a secondary action, `set_value` and an element scroll, not `select_text`. **Adopted.** `clickingMayCauseSelection` (the element or an ancestor below the application is selectable, or lists `AXSelectedRows`, `AXSelectedCells` or `AXSelectedChildren`) only reserves the vendor's route 3, which cua takes for every synthesized click; recorded, not built.
  10. **The menu bar.** A plain left click or `AXPick` on an `AXMenuBarItem` whose children include an `AXMenu` presses nothing and opens nothing: the vendor fakes activation for the observed window and records the item; while it is recorded, observations root the walk at that item (the item, its menu, the menu items), keep the window's header and take no screenshot; the record clears on a successful element click or secondary action, on `AXCancel` of the item, on `menuClosed`, on a new record and on deactivation; no `AXCancel` or Escape is sent. F §5.8 corrected (the walk consumes the recorded item). **Adopted, with no activation**: a background app's menu items are read and pressed through Accessibility without it, and cua's activation is per action; the check that decides it (maws-3f, 2026-10-21) is M2a.5's: on the background TextEdit scene window, an item that AppKit validates against the key window's responder chain (Edit > Select All, or Format > Font > Bold with text selected), its enabled state and its effect compared with the vendor's on the same item; if cua's reads disabled or does nothing, the menu path takes activation, the vendor's held fake activation where cua can hold it without bringing the window to the front, else route 3's per-action activation. Items the app itself handles (About, Settings) cannot show the problem. The vendor's sentence for an unknown secondary action is kept verbatim.
  Still open, run time only: a minimized window's SkyLight capture, the vendor's horizontal scroll, lazily filled menus under the recorded root.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2a.1, intervention, lock screen, route 4, the probe, 2026-10-21; aa3531c0): the gaps the task settled.
  1. **The lock check runs on every `execute`**, observations and every input method included (its refusal carries the input meta on all of them); `ping`, `status`, `approve` and `shutdown` are exempt so the harness and the doctor work while locked.
  2. **The tap's lifetime**: started on the first driven app when the Input Monitoring preflight passes, torn down when no connection drives any app (start and teardown atomic with registering a drive). **The re-query lock** is per connection, a cleared ledger sequence per app: "user changed" means an attributed event newer than the last observation that cleared it, the vendor's sticky flag made per connection. The clear is taken at the tree read and applied only when the observation answers (a failed capture keeps the lock); the debounce's expiry is judged at the read too, so a debounce that expires during the capture leaves the lock set and the next observation clears it, a conservative reading of "expired by the time the observation completes" (the task's review).
  3. **`hidIdleMs`** is the milliseconds since the last event attributed to the driven app, **null when none has been**; with detection off it stays the machine-wide HID idle (Interfaces revised: nullable).
  4. **`entered`** is omitted for a concealed request (it would reveal the secret's length); otherwise it rides on the `type_text` result and on the error that stopped it. In-flight checks run between units and between key presses (never between a key's down and up) and between route 3's activation and its click.
  5. **The call order for every input method**: read the guard (`interventionGuard`), apply the meta, refuse (`checkBeforePosting`), then check in flight between events (`checkInFlight`), so a pre-action refusal still carries its attributed `hidIdleMs`. M2a.2 and M2a.3 keep it.
  6. **Route 4** applies to synthesized clicks only, activates the bound app's process, posts, and restores the previous app and its key window, skipping both when the app is already frontmost; `routes` in `engine.json` accepts only the number 4 per bundle id, anything else stops the engine with exit 78 as `peerPolicy` does; `routeAdvice` counts route 3's guard failures per bundle id engine-wide, a success resetting it, and `status` lists the apps for the doctor. Unmeasured live: whether an `LSUIElement` engine's activation is honoured under macOS 14's cooperative activation, and whether a canvas app needs a pause before the restore (M2a.5).
  7. **The wrapper** tells the two `userIntervened` sentences apart by their fixed openings: the still-interacting refusal always says nothing was entered; the user-changed one depends on whether anything was posted.
  8. **The probe**: `focus`'s `valueHash` is SHA-256 over a string or attributed-string value, null for any other type; `input-source` exits 3 unless the source it reads back is the one it selected.

- Decision (M2a.4, settle, the minimized window, enablement, `cua/serviceMs`, 2026-10-21; 87571ece on `m2a/settle`): the gaps the task settled.
  1. **The open settle** (the vendor-reading entry's item 1) samples every 50 ms, discards its tree and reports `capped` as the action settle does; the request's deadline cancels it as `timeoutReached`.
  2. **The minimized window.** "The tree is not exposed" reads as a minimized window listing no `AXChildren`, checked before the walk and refused as `windowMinimized` (the wrapper admits the name). Otherwise the observation proceeds with no screenshot and `screenshotOmitted: "windowMinimized"` on the Skyshot, surfaced as `cua/screenshotOmitted`; the facade and the model's text are unchanged, and the input refusal is the sentence the model acts on. A click, drag or scroll at a point in a minimized window (an element click judged by the element's own window; a scroll always, being a wheel event at a point) is refused with a sentence naming `setValue`, `selectText` and `performSecondaryAction`. **Keys and typed text are not refused**: they go to the process, whose menu key equivalents act with no window on screen; whether plain typing lands is M2a.5's live check. The live check decides it (maws-3f, 2026-10-21): with the window minimized the hold types into it and reads the document's value through an observation without un-minimizing, and tries one ⌘ equivalent that needs the window (⌘A, the selection read back). If the value is unchanged, `type_text` and a `press_key` with no ⌘ modifier are refused with the minimized sentence when the target's primary window reads minimized (a deterministic precondition on the read already made, unit-tested, needing no second hold), ⌘ chords still passing unless ⌘A also showed no effect, in which case they are refused too; if the value changed, the build stands with the evidence recorded.
  3. **Enablement** is re-read on every observation and set unless it reads true; an unreadable value is set but **not owned**, so never released (the critique's A3 limits ownership to a value cua read as false; corrected from the task's first build by its review); a release that fails keeps ownership for a later retry; ownership persists through later reads of true; the release runs when the last connection that observed the process closes and at shutdown, skips a pid that is now another launch, and forgets the web area so a re-enable waits for the page again.
  4. **`cua/serviceMs`** is the wrapper's time around the whole `handleRpc`, secret read included, on success and on error, set on macOS only (the Linux parity surface stays unchanged). The baseline harness records `cua/engineMs` but not `cua/serviceMs`; M2a.5 adds the one row field (recorded, not judged) so comparison A's transport separation is readable, a harness touch the Plan's "does not touch the harnesses" yields to, since the design names the separation as comparison A's.

  1. **Scroll into view runs before every element action**, route-1 clicks included (F §16.8: the vendor's element click scrolls first whichever route follows, and the route-1 gate's hit-test means something only once the element shows), and does nothing when the element's frame midpoint already shows. Unread in the vendor and chosen here: the scroll-bar fraction from the scroll area's `AXContents` frame (a target before the view aligned to its start, after it to its end, longer than the view to its start, clamped), and a page-scroll secondary action pressing the bar's `AXDecrementPage`/`AXIncrementPage` child before the action itself.
  2. **`set_value`'s coercion** takes an integer field's parsed number as is, parses a date as ISO 8601, and refuses with value-free names (`invalidNumber`, `invalidDate`, `unsupportedValueType`, `valueNotSettable`); the focus fallback click reports the method's route (1) with its guard meta. **`select_text`** searches the Markdown only where the observation would have rendered it (a text field or area, value at most 2,000 UTF-16 units).
  3. **`scroll` with neither element nor point** aims at the primary window's centre (the vendor's virtual cursor has no counterpart); every scroll goes by route 2′ (the vendor activates nothing for a scroll); an element with no visible point is refused as `cannotClickOffscreenElement`.
  4. **The menu-bar record** posts nothing, reports `inputRoute: 1` with a null interval and arms no settle; it needs a plain single left click (any other menu-bar click keeps refusing, the sentence naming the plain click); `menuClosed` clearing is not built, since cua never opens the menu. A menu-rooted observation retires the window's tokens for that revision (N = 1), so the window's indexes renew after the record clears, the vendor's identity rule.
  5. **Route 3's activation spares every non-left synthesized click's target** (right as well as middle): no left event lands on the element's visible frame (for a coordinate click, the hit element's frame, else a 1-point square at the point), generalizing maws-3f's middle-click check.
  6. **The unknown-secondary-action sentence** is finished by the service with the model's index (`<action>` bounded to 256 characters); the engine's own text ends "for the element".
  7. Not built, as M1a left the coordinate click: the vendor's `AXPress` on a menu found under a coordinate click's or drag's point.
  8. **From the review's fixes**: `set_value` re-checks its element after the write matching without the value (F §16.4), taking the replacement handle or failing; when scroll into view reports a scroll, the target is resolved again at one boundary, its owning process re-checked against the forbidden list; the focus fallback click keeps its interval and arms the settle once posted; every write and perform goes through `InputMeta.posting`; `select_text` arms the settle once its write is made, accepted or not. The wrapper admits the names a concealed `set_value` can stop under, `invalidNumber` and `invalidDate` among them: the model learns only that the secret does not parse as the field's type, which it needs to stop retrying it there.

- Decision (M2a.3, paste and `type_text`'s paste route, 2026-10-21; 04b462fc on `m2a/paste`): the gaps the task settled.
  1. **The engine-wide FIFO paste lock** covers route 3's activation as well as snapshot through restore, as the vendor's serial queue does; waiting for it is bounded by the request's deadline (`timeoutReached`), and the in-flight intervention check runs as soon as it is taken.
  2. **The interval** covers only the ⌘V and Return posts (through `InputMeta.posting`): a paste stopped before ⌘V keeps both times null. The activation goes to the app's process and primary window, ⌘V to the process owning the focused element (the vendor's split); a paste's activation failures do not count toward `routeAdvice`.
  3. **`type_text` by paste** pastes each line and presses Return for each newline under one snapshot and one restore, reports `entered`, and checks no source between lines; `paste` carries no `entered`. Every ⌘V and Return press posts all four of its events, so modifiers are always restored.
  4. **New error names**, each with the vendor's sentence verbatim: `clipboardWriteFailed`, `clipboardReadTimedOut`, `clipboardConflict`.
  5. **Formats with Apple frameworks only**: Markdown to HTML through Foundation's Markdown parser and its presentation intents; HTML to RTF and plain text through AppKit's import on the main thread, bounded at 5 s, converted before the pasteboard is touched. Every pasteboard call runs on the main thread. Recorded costs: the HTML import may fetch an `<img>` URL inside the bound, and the first `html` or `md` paste after an engine launch takes about 4 s while the import warms up.

- Decision (menu activation held from the record, 2026-10-21, maws-3f; revises the M2a vendor reading's departure "the menu bar is recorded with no activation"): **cua takes the vendor's held activation for a background app's menu.**
  **The hold's evidence** (M2a.5 hold item 3; `m2a/live/menu/result.json`): in a background TextEdit, the vendor's Edit > Select All and Format > Font > Bold both acted and read enabled. cua's results:
  - switch off: Select All read disabled, and Bold was not found;
  - switch on, with route 3's activation around the press: both still read disabled and had no effect (route 1, guard 36 and 54 ms).
  **Why per-action activation failed:** it came after the menu had already worked out its enablement. The app computes item enablement when the recorded menu is observed, and the vendor's activation is already held at that point. Without it, cua's menu commands did nothing on a background app while the vendor's worked: a functional regression, and parity wins.
  **The owner's front is never taken.** The fake activation sends the app an activation event and keeps the user's app in front; it is not a real app switch.
  - **Trigger and span as the vendor's** (vendor reading §4, items 2 and 6). Route 3's fake activation (`safelyEnforceActiveState`; the user's front app is kept in front) runs only at the menu-bar record: a plain click or AXPick on an AXMenuBarItem with an AXMenu child. It never runs on an ordinary observation. It holds through the recorded menu's observations and the item press.
  - **Release, a declared difference in the owner's favour.** The vendor deactivates only at its lifecycle end. cua posts route 3's deactivation when the menu interaction ends: after the item press, at the first request aimed elsewhere (`resolve_app` included), when the record clears, or after a **120 s idle bound** (revised by maws-3f from 10 s).
  **Why 120 s.** Holding costs almost nothing: the activation is fake, the owner's front is never taken, and the vendor holds forever. Releasing early costs the feature, because a press that arrives after one slow model turn does nothing, and thinking turns often run 30–90 s. The other three release points end almost every real interaction; the bound only catches an abandoned menu, and for that case 120 s against 30 s makes no difference to the owner. A press after an idle release is never a silent no-op: it gets a clear answer and presses nothing.
  **Activations are shared per target process and reference-counted across connections** (af91e109), so one connection's release never withdraws another's held menu. Front-app restore is activation-only. No AX touches any other app.
  - **Verification.** Unit tests cover the span and the release. The live check is in M2b's matrix against the vendor, on Select All and Bold. `spikeMenuActivation` is removed (9c58dd0e).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2b pre-flight, 2026-10-21, on branch `feat/117-m2b` from main 885d7d8e; brief from maws-3f): M2b runs as six tasks (Progress), and M2a's residue (its report's Residue section, the "M2a.5 hold" entry, the held menu activation) becomes matrix cells. Gaps settled in the spirit of the design:
  1. **The first hold comes first and may precede the matrix.** M2b.1a adds a key-code-only variant of `--probe keystroke` (`--key-codes-only`: the same key codes and flags from the current layout, no `CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString`) and runs the HID composition row in both variants in one hold under Korean 2-Set, the TextEdit scene frontmost and key, recording per variant the AX value (가, rk or ㄱㅏ), the probe's JSON and the layout id, beside paste live. MAWS P4-B2 reads it: with key codes only, `rk` means the input method was bypassed and 가 that it composed. M2b.1b adds the rest of the residue on the same live harness (`scripts/native-matrix/live*.mjs`, M2a.5a's): route 4's live cells, `select_text` on a minimized window read a second time so the write and the read are told apart, the enablement release judged by Chromium's own accessibility mode (never the attribute's readback), the held menu activation against the vendor on Select All and Bold (replacing the switch-on lane, whose switch is gone), and the horizontal scroll against the vendor's one-wheel scroll (item 6 of "M2a vendor reading"). These cells are also reachable as `native-matrix.mjs cell <name>`, and `summary` reads their results.
  2. **Route 4 is driven without the owner's `~/.config/cua/engine.json`**: through a prepared home the engine is launched under, or through the request, whichever the engine's launch path allows without touching that file on the mini.
  3. **The matrix drives both lanes through the real facade on a host** (`--host`, `--engine`), as the baseline harness does, so its speed cells are in-cell; the device-side oracles (front window, activation watcher, `--probe focus`, `--probe keystroke`) are separate processes, as part 5 defines them. M2b.2 builds the framework, the method matrix in both regimes, the speed and finding-elements cells, the non-interference oracle with the key guard's user-window case, `compare` and `summary`; M2b.3 adds the probe-driven cells (concurrent use and its sheet variant, the two positive intervention cells), the Korean and Unicode matrix with its source and per-context save and restore, and the settle cell; M2b.4 the `native-mac` parity suite with `--engine`, the approved-runtime and approved-engine route on macOS, the darwin native command and the modulo-renumbering diff with its `summary` line. M2b.4 touches disjoint files and runs beside M2b.2 and M2b.3 in its own worktree.
  4. **Holds.** Every GUI step on the mini runs inside a CI-opt hold maws-3f books, from the checkout `~/Developer/GitHub/cua-parity`, with `pgrep -x SecurityAgent` and `pgrep -f 'claude auth status'` empty before it starts (friction tracker #177), and ends with the put-back M2a.5 defined (engine stopped, no scene app or Chrome for Testing, Finder's keys and in-memory chrome, the input source and per-context preference, the front app by `lsappinfo`), whose readings go to maws-3f, who releases the hold.
  5. **Review voiding.** A hold's result is voided only by a finding that changes what the measured path binds, captures or returns; every other finding is fixed afterwards or logged in `tech-debt-tracker.md`. A whole-milestone reviewer-high runs before the PR, which lands by squash, references #117 and carries no Closes line.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision: no cursor overlay, no per-app instruction texts, no cross-connection approval persistence, no audio, no lock-screen unlock in this work.
  Rationale: none is reachable by cua's facade today or wanted by the owner; MAWS synthesizes its own agent cursor.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.

## Outcomes & Retrospective

Pending for the initiative — written at finish. M1a's interim account (2026-10-20) follows; the full record is `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-18-own-native-engine-m1a-report.md`.

**M1a (a807827a..b2ccdc3b, nothing run live).** The engine core, basic input and the service landed as six tasks, each executor-built and reviewed to clean: M1a.1 (core), M1a.2 (input), M1a.2b (derivations the goldens refuted, added mid-milestone), M1a.3 (renderer, diff, goldens), M1a.4 (`src/` integration and the ci-kit Swift gate), M1a.5 (protocol, client, policy, numbering, result meta, the untrusted path). The whole-branch review (reviewer-high, a807827a..bd1136f1) found the branch correct with three Minor findings: two fixed (6f110605), one a tech-debt row. Hosted CI carries `engine-swift` (Swift build and unit tests) beside `test`.

What went as designed: the per-lineage token model, the routes the second sitting measured, and the renderer's goldens comparison modulo renumbering held through every review; the seams (synthetic AX trees, recorded fixtures, a fake engine) let every derivation and input decision be tested without a GUI.

What the milestone taught:
- The goldens are the strongest evidence M1a has, and they refuted the engine more than the renderer: M1a.3's first run showed derivations ported by reading the vendor's output rather than its code. M1a.2b re-took each predicate from the decompiled function that produces it, with its address, and the remaining mismatches are declared engine-fault corrections that expire when M1b re-records the goldens live. Rule: port a derivation from the code that produces it, never fit it to recorded output.
- The hosted runner grants the engine Accessibility, against this spec's assumption; the untrusted real-engine path runs nowhere, and the fake engine carries it. Live AX observation on a hosted runner is a follow-up.
- A gate whose path filter the milestone touched only late ran only at the end: `sandbox-live`'s probe-secrets still compared a substituted request against the bare input after M1a.4 added the `concealed` mark, and failed on bd1136f1 (fixed in b2ccdc3b). A change to a wire shape should run every gate whose `paths` it matches before the frontier review, not after.

Left for M1b: re-record the goldens live from the M1a.2b engine and delete every correction; Finder compared as full text; the live checks listed in the M1a report's "Unverified".


**M1b (a254a1f0..HEAD, closed 2026-10-21 05:40Z).** The code (M1b.1–M1b.4b) landed reviewed; the live sitting took four holds on the mini. All three Proves lines are met on the approved build 4b31b8e61195: **comparison A passes** (3 vv + 3 vc, `compare --engines` PASS), **the selection table runs live** (303 rows), and **the four goldens are re-recorded live and installed with every engine-fault correction expired**, Finder compared as full text (`goldens.mjs check` exit 0; one line declared as the vendor's capped count). The goldens took three holds: the first found Finder's macOS 27 status bar and Chrome's filling page, the second found that Finder's toggle state and its window disagree (so its chrome is now read off the window), the third recorded all four once a frame comparison stopped depending on JSON key order. Account: `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-18-own-native-engine-m1b-report.md` and its addendum.

What the milestone taught:
- The live comparison earned its place: it found a vendor behaviour no fixture showed (the reopen of a windowless running app), and the vendor's decompile settled the fix in minutes where reasoning from symptoms had produced a wrong hypothesis (an activation).
- Every detector built from one sitting's evidence needs its negative case recorded too: the capture watch's `SCAlert` substring matched replayd's per-stream bookkeeping, and the 60 s `spawnSync` default killed `codesign` under the owner's prompt.
- A harness assumption about the OS (Finder's unset defaults, `ps` etime's rounding, Chrome's readiness) is a defect waiting for a new OS or a faster Mac; each surfaced only on the device.
- Reviewing a fix while its runs go is cheap only with a stated convergence rule; the fourth round converged on the rule "void only for the measured path".
- A harness that decides from a reading of the device's state (a preference key, an OS default) must check the state it acts on, not the reading: Finder's keys predicted neither what its window drew nor what its toggle would do, and the fix was to observe the window before and after each toggle and to restore what the owner saw, not what the keys said.
- An audit's rule needs its negative direction tested: the expiry audit counted a correction that created a difference as needed, and labelled an expired correction an engine defect.
- Parity with the vendor's text is not the goal where the vendor's text is wrong: its item count is capped by a prefetch limit, and cua keeps the true count with the line declared as the vendor's defect.

**M2a (5941e988..HEAD on feat/117-m2a; hold 2026-10-21 12:28–13:22Z).** The full method set, settle and intervention landed as four engine lanes and a hold harness. Each lane was reviewed to clean (M2a.3 took four rounds, the harness six), then integrated. A whole-milestone reviewer-high round found three Important and one Minor finding, all fixed and re-reviewed correct. The hold ran on build 1ec58faab897:
- comparison A passed, with cua's task rows at or below the vendor's (`task.total` 3126 against 4384 ms);
- the minimized, menu, input-source and enablement checks ran;
- paste and the HID composition row did not run, because an unrelated console prompt was present.
The full record is `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-18-own-native-engine-m2a-report.md`.

What the milestone taught:
- **A live comparison against the vendor settles questions that reading its code leaves open.** The decompile said the vendor's only menu difference was "held activation". It took the hold to show that this difference decides whether a background menu works at all: the menu works out enablement before an activation at the press arrives.
- **A per-call report of what was sent is not evidence that it landed.** type_text into a minimized window reported every unit entered while the value did not change. Checks that decide policy have to read the effect, which is what the hold's checks did.
- **A harness that touches the owner's machine needs its abort path reviewed as hard as its measurements.** The first harness review found a Critical: window titles and text could leave the device. Three rounds then went to recovery ownership. The convergence rule that ended the loop was "abort-path gaps go to tech debt once the hold is attended and an independent put-back check backs it". Agree on that rule before the first review round, not after the fourth.
- **The device is shared, and its other tenants surface as the harness's own failures.** The owner's menubar app raised a keychain prompt, which aborted the paste section and hung codesign. The hung codesign then made `cua engine status` report a false negative. Status code that reads the environment has to distinguish "could not read" from "read no".
- **An attribute's readback is not the state it controls.** On Chrome, AppKit answers the enablement attribute's readback, not Chromium's mode, so verifying the release needs Chromium's own signal.

Left for M2b:
- the paste and HID composition rows;
- route 4's live cells;
- the held menu activation, checked live against the vendor;
- select_text in a minimized window;
- verifying enablement's release by Chromium's mode;
- the vendor-parity menu items logged in tech-debt-tracker.md.
